+//! Cluster tests: a few replicas and a client driven by hand through a
+//! tick function that decides which messages get delivered.
+
+use parking_lot::Mutex;
+use std::cell::RefCell;
+use std::sync::Arc;
+use vsr_rs::{Client, Config, Message, Replica, StateMachine};
+
+#[test]
+fn test_normal_operation() {
+    let _ = env_logger::try_init();
+    let (client_tx, _client_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let (replica_tx, replica_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let config = Arc::new(Config::new());
+    let sm = Arc::new(Accumulator::new());
+    let a_id = config.add_replica();
+    let replica_a = Replica::new(
+        a_id,
+        config.clone(),
+        sm.clone(),
+        client_tx.clone(),
+        replica_tx.clone(),
+    );
+    let b_id = config.add_replica();
+    let replica_b = Replica::new(
+        b_id,
+        config.clone(),
+        Arc::new(Accumulator::new()),
+        client_tx.clone(),
+        replica_tx.clone(),
+    );
+    let c_id = config.add_replica();
+    let replica_c = Replica::new(
+        c_id,
+        config.clone(),
+        Arc::new(Accumulator::new()),
+        client_tx,
+        replica_tx.clone(),
+    );
+    let replicas = vec![replica_a, replica_b, replica_c];
+    let tick = || {
+        while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+            let (replica_id, message) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+            replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+        }
+    };
+    let client = Client::new(config, replica_tx);
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(10), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick();
+    let accumulator = (sm.accumulator.lock()).to_owned();
+    assert_eq!(10, accumulator);
+    client.on_request(Op::Sub(5), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick();
+    let accumulator = (sm.accumulator.lock()).to_owned();
+    assert_eq!(5, accumulator);
+}
+#[test]
+fn test_idle() {
+    let _ = env_logger::try_init();
+    let (client_tx, _client_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let (replica_tx, replica_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let config = Arc::new(Config::new());
+    let sm = Arc::new(Accumulator::new());
+    let a_id = config.add_replica();
+    let replica_a = Replica::new(
+        a_id,
+        config.clone(),
+        sm.clone(),
+        client_tx.clone(),
+        replica_tx.clone(),
+    );
+    let sm_b = Arc::new(Accumulator::new());
+    let b_id = config.add_replica();
+    let replica_b = Replica::new(
+        b_id,
+        config.clone(),
+        sm_b,
+        client_tx.clone(),
+        replica_tx.clone(),
+    );
+    let sm_c = Arc::new(Accumulator::new());
+    let c_id = config.add_replica();
+    let replica_c = Replica::new(c_id, config.clone(), sm_c, client_tx, replica_tx.clone());
+    let replicas = vec![replica_a, replica_b, replica_c];
+    let tick = || {
+        while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+            let (replica_id, message) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+            replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+        }
+        for replica in &replicas {
+            replica.on_idle();
+        }
+    };
+    let client = Client::new(config, replica_tx);
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(10), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick();
+    let accumulator = (sm.accumulator.lock()).to_owned();
+    assert_eq!(10, accumulator);
+    client.on_request(Op::Sub(5), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick();
+    let accumulator = (sm.accumulator.lock()).to_owned();
+    assert_eq!(5, accumulator);
+    tick();
+    let accumulator_b = (sm.accumulator.lock()).to_owned();
+    let accumulator_c = (sm.accumulator.lock()).to_owned();
+    assert_eq!(accumulator, accumulator_b);
+    assert_eq!(accumulator, accumulator_c);
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn test_recovery() {
+    let _ = env_logger::try_init();
+    let (client_tx, _client_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let (replica_tx, replica_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let config = Arc::new(Config::new());
+    let sm = Arc::new(Accumulator::new());
+    let a_id = config.add_replica();
+    let replica_a = Replica::new(
+        a_id,
+        config.clone(),
+        sm.clone(),
+        client_tx.clone(),
+        replica_tx.clone(),
+    );
+    let b_id = config.add_replica();
+    let replica_b = Replica::new(
+        b_id,
+        config.clone(),
+        Arc::new(Accumulator::new()),
+        client_tx.clone(),
+        replica_tx.clone(),
+    );
+    let c_id = config.add_replica();
+    let replica_c = Replica::new(
+        c_id,
+        config.clone(),
+        Arc::new(Accumulator::new()),
+        client_tx,
+        replica_tx.clone(),
+    );
+    let replicas = vec![replica_a, replica_b, replica_c];
+    let tick = || {
+        while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+            let (replica_id, message) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+            replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+        }
+    };
+    let tick_lossy = || {
+        while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+            let (replica_id, message) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+            if replica_id == 1 {
+                continue;
+            }
+            replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+        }
+    };
+    let client = Client::new(config, replica_tx);
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(10), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick_lossy();
+    let accumulator = (sm.accumulator.lock()).to_owned();
+    assert_eq!(10, accumulator);
+    client.on_request(Op::Sub(5), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick();
+    let accumulator = (sm.accumulator.lock()).to_owned();
+    assert_eq!(5, accumulator);
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(7), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick();
+    let accumulator = (sm.accumulator.lock()).to_owned();
+    assert_eq!(12, accumulator);
+}
+
+/// Prepare messages to one backup arrive in reverse order. The first
+/// one it sees has a gap, so it starts state transfer; the earlier ones
+/// then arrive while it is still waiting for `NewState` and must be
+/// dropped, otherwise its log moves past the point it asked to be
+/// repaired from.
+#[test]
+fn test_prepare_reordered_during_state_transfer() {
+    let _ = env_logger::try_init();
+    let (client_tx, _client_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let (replica_tx, replica_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let config = Arc::new(Config::new());
+    let sms: Vec<_> = (0..3).map(|_| Arc::new(Accumulator::new())).collect();
+    let replicas: Vec<_> = sms
+        .iter()
+        .map(|sm| {
+            let id = config.add_replica();
+            Replica::new(
+                id,
+                config.clone(),
+                sm.clone(),
+                client_tx.clone(),
+                replica_tx.clone(),
+            )
+        })
+        .collect();
+    // Delivers everything, but `Prepare` messages to replica 1 in
+    // descending op-number order.
+    let tick_reordered = || {
+        while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+            let mut batch: Vec<_> = replica_rx.try_iter().collect();
+            batch.sort_by_key(|(replica_id, message)| match message {
+                Message::Prepare { op_number, .. } if *replica_id == 1 => {
+                    std::cmp::Reverse(*op_number)
+                }
+                _ => std::cmp::Reverse(0),
+            });
+            for (replica_id, message) in batch {
+                replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+            }
+        }
+    };
+    let tick = || {
+        while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+            let (replica_id, message) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+            replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+        }
+    };
+    let client = Client::new(config, replica_tx);
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(10), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(20), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(30), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick_reordered();
+    assert_eq!(60, *sms[0].accumulator.lock());
+    assert_eq!(3, replicas[1].op_number());
+    // A commit heartbeat lets the backups catch up.
+    for replica in &replicas {
+        replica.on_idle();
+    }
+    tick();
+    for sm in &sms {
+        assert_eq!(60, *sm.accumulator.lock());
+    }
+}
+
+/// The acknowledgements for op 1 are lost, but both backups acknowledge
+/// op 2. A `PrepareOk` for op n acknowledges n and all earlier ops, so
+/// the primary must commit ops 1 and 2, in order, once op 2 reaches a
+/// quorum, instead of executing op 2 in op 1's slot.
+#[test]
+fn test_prepare_ok_quorum_commits_prefix() {
+    let _ = env_logger::try_init();
+    let (client_tx, _client_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let (replica_tx, replica_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let config = Arc::new(Config::new());
+    let sms: Vec<_> = (0..3).map(|_| Arc::new(Accumulator::new())).collect();
+    let replicas: Vec<_> = sms
+        .iter()
+        .map(|sm| {
+            let id = config.add_replica();
+            Replica::new(
+                id,
+                config.clone(),
+                sm.clone(),
+                client_tx.clone(),
+                replica_tx.clone(),
+            )
+        })
+        .collect();
+    let tick_drop_prepare_ok_1 = || {
+        while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+            let (replica_id, message) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+            if matches!(message, Message::PrepareOk { op_number: 1, .. }) {
+                continue;
+            }
+            replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+        }
+    };
+    let tick = || {
+        while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+            let (replica_id, message) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+            replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+        }
+    };
+    let client = Client::new(config, replica_tx);
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(10), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(20), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick_drop_prepare_ok_1();
+    assert_eq!(2, replicas[0].commit_number());
+    assert_eq!(30, *sms[0].accumulator.lock());
+    // A commit heartbeat lets the backups catch up.
+    for replica in &replicas {
+        replica.on_idle();
+    }
+    tick();
+    for sm in &sms {
+        assert_eq!(30, *sm.accumulator.lock());
+    }
+}
+
+/// A backup's `GetState` is replayed by the network. The reply to the
+/// replayed copy arrives during a later state transfer, when the backup
+/// already has more entries than the reply starts at. The stale reply
+/// overlaps the backup's log and carries the entries it is missing, so
+/// the backup must use the suffix instead of treating the mismatch as
+/// a fatal error.
+#[test]
+fn test_stale_new_state_during_state_transfer() {
+    let _ = env_logger::try_init();
+    let (client_tx, _client_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let (replica_tx, replica_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let config = Arc::new(Config::new());
+    let sms: Vec<_> = (0..3).map(|_| Arc::new(Accumulator::new())).collect();
+    let replicas: Vec<_> = sms
+        .iter()
+        .map(|sm| {
+            let id = config.add_replica();
+            Replica::new(
+                id,
+                config.clone(),
+                sm.clone(),
+                client_tx.clone(),
+                replica_tx.clone(),
+            )
+        })
+        .collect();
+    // Delivers every message for which `deliver` returns true.
+    let tick_with = |deliver: &dyn Fn(usize, &Message<Op>) -> bool| {
+        while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+            let (replica_id, message) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+            if deliver(replica_id, &message) {
+                replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+            }
+        }
+    };
+    let replayed = RefCell::new(Vec::new());
+    let client = Client::new(config, replica_tx);
+
+    // Replica 1 misses op 2, so op 3 makes it ask for state transfer.
+    // The network replays its `GetState`; keep the copy for later.
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(10), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(20), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(30), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick_with(&|replica_id, message| match message {
+        Message::Prepare { op_number: 2, .. } if replica_id == 1 => false,
+        Message::GetState { .. } => {
+            replayed.borrow_mut().push((replica_id, message.clone()));
+            true
+        }
+        _ => true,
+    });
+    assert_eq!(3, replicas[1].op_number());
+
+    // Replica 1 misses op 4, so op 5 makes it ask for state transfer
+    // again, but this time the `GetState` is lost.
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(40), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(50), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick_with(&|replica_id, message| match message {
+        Message::Prepare { op_number: 4, .. } if replica_id == 1 => false,
+        Message::GetState { .. } => false,
+        _ => true,
+    });
+    assert_eq!(3, replicas[1].op_number());
+
+    // The replayed `GetState` from the first transfer now reaches the
+    // primary. Its reply starts at op 1 and covers ops 1 to 5, and it
+    // reaches replica 1 while it waits for a reply starting at op 3.
+    for (replica_id, message) in replayed.borrow_mut().drain(..) {
+        replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+    }
+    tick_with(&|_, _| true);
+    assert_eq!(5, replicas[1].op_number());
+
+    // A commit heartbeat lets the backups catch up.
+    for replica in &replicas {
+        replica.on_idle();
+    }
+    tick_with(&|_, _| true);
+    for sm in &sms {
+        assert_eq!(150, *sm.accumulator.lock());
+    }
+}
+
+/// A backup's `GetState` is lost. On the next idle period it must ask
+/// again instead of staying in state transfer forever.
+#[test]
+fn test_get_state_retried_on_idle() {
+    let _ = env_logger::try_init();
+    let (client_tx, _client_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let (replica_tx, replica_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let config = Arc::new(Config::new());
+    let sms: Vec<_> = (0..3).map(|_| Arc::new(Accumulator::new())).collect();
+    let replicas: Vec<_> = sms
+        .iter()
+        .map(|sm| {
+            let id = config.add_replica();
+            Replica::new(
+                id,
+                config.clone(),
+                sm.clone(),
+                client_tx.clone(),
+                replica_tx.clone(),
+            )
+        })
+        .collect();
+    let tick_with = |deliver: &dyn Fn(usize, &Message<Op>) -> bool| {
+        while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+            let (replica_id, message) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+            if deliver(replica_id, &message) {
+                replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+            }
+        }
+    };
+    let idle = || {
+        for replica in &replicas {
+            replica.on_idle();
+        }
+    };
+    let client = Client::new(config, replica_tx);
+    // Replica 1 misses op 2, asks for state transfer, and the request
+    // is lost.
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(10), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(20), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(30), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick_with(&|replica_id, message| match message {
+        Message::Prepare { op_number: 2, .. } if replica_id == 1 => false,
+        Message::GetState { .. } => false,
+        _ => true,
+    });
+    assert_eq!(1, replicas[1].op_number());
+    // The idle period must make it ask again.
+    idle();
+    tick_with(&|_, _| true);
+    assert_eq!(3, replicas[1].op_number());
+    idle();
+    tick_with(&|_, _| true);
+    for sm in &sms {
+        assert_eq!(60, *sm.accumulator.lock());
+    }
+}
+
+/// Every `PrepareOk` for the last ops is lost. On the next idle period
+/// the primary must re-send the uncommitted `Prepare` messages so the
+/// backups acknowledge them again.
+#[test]
+fn test_prepare_resent_on_idle_when_prepare_ok_lost() {
+    let _ = env_logger::try_init();
+    let (client_tx, _client_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let (replica_tx, replica_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let config = Arc::new(Config::new());
+    let sms: Vec<_> = (0..3).map(|_| Arc::new(Accumulator::new())).collect();
+    let replicas: Vec<_> = sms
+        .iter()
+        .map(|sm| {
+            let id = config.add_replica();
+            Replica::new(
+                id,
+                config.clone(),
+                sm.clone(),
+                client_tx.clone(),
+                replica_tx.clone(),
+            )
+        })
+        .collect();
+    let tick_with = |deliver: &dyn Fn(usize, &Message<Op>) -> bool| {
+        while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+            let (replica_id, message) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+            if deliver(replica_id, &message) {
+                replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+            }
+        }
+    };
+    let idle = || {
+        for replica in &replicas {
+            replica.on_idle();
+        }
+    };
+    let client = Client::new(config, replica_tx);
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(10), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(20), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick_with(&|_, message| !matches!(message, Message::PrepareOk { .. }));
+    assert_eq!(2, replicas[1].op_number());
+    assert_eq!(2, replicas[2].op_number());
+    assert_eq!(0, replicas[0].commit_number());
+    // The idle period must get the ops acknowledged and committed.
+    idle();
+    tick_with(&|_, _| true);
+    assert_eq!(2, replicas[0].commit_number());
+    idle();
+    tick_with(&|_, _| true);
+    for sm in &sms {
+        assert_eq!(30, *sm.accumulator.lock());
+    }
+}
+
+/// The `Prepare` for the last op never reaches any backup, so no backup
+/// can notice a gap. On the next idle period the primary must re-send
+/// it.
+#[test]
+fn test_prepare_resent_on_idle_when_prepare_lost() {
+    let _ = env_logger::try_init();
+    let (client_tx, _client_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let (replica_tx, replica_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let config = Arc::new(Config::new());
+    let sms: Vec<_> = (0..3).map(|_| Arc::new(Accumulator::new())).collect();
+    let replicas: Vec<_> = sms
+        .iter()
+        .map(|sm| {
+            let id = config.add_replica();
+            Replica::new(
+                id,
+                config.clone(),
+                sm.clone(),
+                client_tx.clone(),
+                replica_tx.clone(),
+            )
+        })
+        .collect();
+    let tick_with = |deliver: &dyn Fn(usize, &Message<Op>) -> bool| {
+        while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+            let (replica_id, message) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+            if deliver(replica_id, &message) {
+                replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+            }
+        }
+    };
+    let idle = || {
+        for replica in &replicas {
+            replica.on_idle();
+        }
+    };
+    let client = Client::new(config, replica_tx);
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(10), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(20), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick_with(&|_, message| !matches!(message, Message::Prepare { op_number: 2, .. }));
+    assert_eq!(1, replicas[1].op_number());
+    assert_eq!(1, replicas[2].op_number());
+    assert_eq!(1, replicas[0].commit_number());
+    // The idle period must deliver op 2 and get it committed.
+    idle();
+    tick_with(&|_, _| true);
+    assert_eq!(2, replicas[0].commit_number());
+    idle();
+    tick_with(&|_, _| true);
+    for sm in &sms {
+        assert_eq!(30, *sm.accumulator.lock());
+    }
+}
+
+#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
+enum Op {
+    Add(i32),
+    Sub(i32),
+}
+
+struct Accumulator {
+    accumulator: Mutex<i32>,
+}
+
+impl Accumulator {
+    fn new() -> Accumulator {
+        let accumulator = Mutex::new(0);
+        Accumulator { accumulator }
+    }
+}
+
+impl StateMachine for Accumulator {
+    type Input = Op;
+    type Output = ();
+
+    fn apply(&self, op: Op) {
+        let mut accumulator = self.accumulator.lock();
+        match op {
+            Op::Add(value) => {
+                *accumulator += value;
+            }
+            Op::Sub(value) => {
+                *accumulator -= value;
+            }
+        }
+    }
+}

COMMIT 9176a52743c150fe009da1fbe0f4b41107205364
Collapse the library into a single src/lib.rs

Five of the seven source files were under 70 lines, and everything
outside the crate already went through the re-exports, so the split
bought nothing but indirection. The whole library is under 600 lines
and reads better top to bottom: types, state machine trait, config,
messages, client, replica. Split again along real seams once view
changes and recovery make it grow.

The type aliases that used to live in a private module are now public,
which they effectively were already through the public signatures that
used them. No code changes otherwise.

-pub mod client;

+//! Viewstamped Replication for Rust.
+//!
+//! A work-in-progress implementation of the protocol described in
+//! "Viewstamped Replication Revisited" by Liskov and Cowling.
+
+use crossbeam_channel::Sender;
+use log::trace;
+use std::cell::{Ref, RefCell};
+use std::collections::HashMap;
+use std::fmt::Debug;
+use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicUsize, Ordering};
+use std::sync::Arc;
+
+pub type ClientID = usize;
+pub type CommitID = usize;
+pub type OpNumber = usize;
+pub type ReplicaID = usize;
+pub type RequestNumber = usize;
+pub type ViewNumber = usize;
+
+/// State machine.
+pub trait StateMachine {
+    type Input: Clone + Debug + Send;
+    type Output;
+
+    fn apply(&self, input: Self::Input) -> Self::Output;
+}
+
+/// Configuration.
+#[derive(Debug, Default)]
+pub struct Config {
+    /// IDs of all replicas (in sorted order).
+    pub replicas: RefCell<Vec<ReplicaID>>,
+}
+
+impl Config {
+    pub fn new() -> Config {
+        let replicas = RefCell::new(Vec::default());
+        Config { replicas }
+    }
+
+    pub fn primary_id(&self, view_number: ViewNumber) -> ReplicaID {
+        let replicas = self.replicas.borrow();
+        let idx = view_number % replicas.len();
+        replicas[idx]
+    }
+
+    pub fn add_replica(&self) -> usize {
+        let mut replicas = self.replicas.borrow_mut();
+        let id = replicas.len();
+        replicas.push(id);
+        id
+    }
+
+    pub fn quorum(&self) -> usize {
+        let replicas = self.replicas.borrow();
+        replicas.len() / 2 + 1
+    }
+}
+
+#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
+pub enum Message<Op>
+where
+    Op: Clone + Debug + Send,
+{
+    Request {
+        client_id: ClientID,
+        request_number: RequestNumber,
+        op: Op,
+    },
+    Prepare {
+        view_number: ViewNumber,
+        op_number: OpNumber,
+        op: Op,
+        commit_number: CommitID,
+    },
+    PrepareOk {
+        view_number: ViewNumber,
+        op_number: OpNumber,
+    },
+    Commit {
+        view_number: ViewNumber,
+        commit_number: CommitID,
+    },
+    GetState {
+        replica_id: ReplicaID,
+        view_number: ViewNumber,
+        op_number: OpNumber,
+    },
+    /// The NewState message is sent to a replica to repair it.
+    ///
+    /// We differ from the paper by not just sending the op number of the last
+    /// entry in the log, but also the op number of the first entry. This allows
+    /// us to verify that we're repairing the right part of the log in the
+    /// replica.
+    NewState {
+        /// The view number of the replica that is sending the NewState message.
+        view_number: ViewNumber,
+        /// The log of operations that this replica needs to apply to catch up.
+        log: Vec<Op>,
+        /// The op number of the first entry in the log. This is the op number
+        /// that we requested in the GetState message.
+        op_number_start: OpNumber,
+        /// The op number of the last entry in the log.
+        op_number_end: OpNumber,
+        /// The commit ID of the replica that is sending the NewState message.
+        commit_number: CommitID,
+    },
+}
+
+pub type ClientCallback = Box<dyn Fn(RequestNumber) + Send>;
+
+/// Client.
+pub struct Client<Op>
+where
+    Op: Clone + Debug + Send,
+{
+    config: Arc<Config>,
+    client_id: ClientID,
+    view_number: ViewNumber,
+    replica_tx: Sender<(ReplicaID, Message<Op>)>,
+    request_number: AtomicUsize,
+    callbacks: RefCell<Option<(RequestNumber, ClientCallback)>>,
+}
+
+impl<Op> Client<Op>
+where
+    Op: Clone + Debug + Send,
+{
+    pub fn new(config: Arc<Config>, replica_tx: Sender<(ReplicaID, Message<Op>)>) -> Client<Op> {
+        let request_number = AtomicUsize::new(0);
+        let callbacks = RefCell::new(None);
+        Client {
+            config,
+            client_id: 0,
+            view_number: 0,
+            replica_tx,
+            request_number,
+            callbacks,
+        }
+    }
+
+    pub fn on_request(&self, op: Op, callback: ClientCallback) {
+        trace!("Client {} <- {:?}", self.client_id, op);
+        let primary_id = self.config.primary_id(self.view_number);
+        let request_number = self.request_number.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);
+        self.callbacks.replace(Some((request_number, callback)));
+        self.replica_tx
+            .send((
+                primary_id,
+                Message::Request {
+                    client_id: self.client_id,
+                    request_number,
+                    op,
+                },
+            ))
+            .unwrap();
+    }
+
+    pub fn on_message(&self) {
+        let mut callbacks = self.callbacks.borrow_mut();
+        if let Some((request_number, callback)) = callbacks.take() {
+            callback(request_number);
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+/// Replica status.
+#[derive(Debug, PartialEq)]
+enum Status {
+    Normal,
+    Recovery,
+}
+
+#[derive(Debug)]
+pub struct Replica<SM: StateMachine> {
+    config: Arc<Config>,
+    self_id: ReplicaID,
+    state_machine: Arc<SM>,
+    status: RefCell<Status>,
+    view_number: ViewNumber,
+    commit_number: AtomicUsize,
+    op_number: AtomicUsize,
+    log: RefCell<Vec<SM::Input>>,
+    acks: RefCell<HashMap<ReplicaID, usize>>,
+    client_tx: Sender<SM::Output>,
+    replica_tx: Sender<(ReplicaID, Message<SM::Input>)>,
+}
+
+impl<SM: StateMachine> Replica<SM> {
+    pub fn new(
+        self_id: ReplicaID,
+        config: Arc<Config>,
+        state_machine: Arc<SM>,
+        client_tx: Sender<SM::Output>,
+        replica_tx: Sender<(ReplicaID, Message<SM::Input>)>,
+    ) -> Replica<SM> {
+        let status = RefCell::new(Status::Normal);
+        let view_number = 0;
+        let commit_number = AtomicUsize::new(0);
+        let op_number = AtomicUsize::new(0);
+        let log = RefCell::new(Vec::default());
+        let acks = RefCell::new(HashMap::default());
+        Replica {
+            self_id,
+            config,
+            state_machine,
+            status,
+            view_number,
+            commit_number,
+            op_number,
+            log,
+            acks,
+            client_tx,
+            replica_tx,
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// The main entry point to replica logic.
+    pub fn on_message(&self, message: Message<SM::Input>) {
+        trace!("Replica {} <- {:?}", self.self_id, message);
+        match message {
+            Message::Request { op, .. } => {
+                self.on_request(op);
+            }
+            Message::Prepare {
+                view_number,
+                op_number,
+                op,
+                commit_number,
+            } => {
+                self.on_prepare(view_number, op_number, op, commit_number);
+            }
+            Message::PrepareOk {
+                view_number,
+                op_number,
+            } => {
+                self.on_prepare_ok(view_number, op_number);
+            }
+            Message::Commit {
+                view_number,
+                commit_number,
+            } => {
+                self.on_commit(view_number, commit_number);
+            }
+            Message::GetState {
+                replica_id,
+                view_number,
+                op_number,
+            } => {
+                self.on_get_state(replica_id, view_number, op_number);
+            }
+            Message::NewState {
+                view_number,
+                log,
+                op_number_start,
+                op_number_end,
+                commit_number,
+            } => {
+                self.on_new_state(
+                    view_number,
+                    log,
+                    op_number_start,
+                    op_number_end,
+                    commit_number,
+                );
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// The client sends a `Request` message to the primary, which replicates
+    /// the operation to the other replicas.
+    fn on_request(&self, op: SM::Input) {
+        // TODO: If not primary, drop request, advise client to connect to primary.
+        assert!(self.is_primary());
+        // TODO: If not in normal status, drop request, advise client to try later.
+        assert_eq!(*self.status.borrow(), Status::Normal);
+        // Append operation to our log.
+        self.append_to_log(op.clone());
+        // And then register our own acknowledgement.
+        let op_number = self.op_number();
+        let mut acks = self.acks.borrow_mut();
+        acks.insert(op_number, 1);
+        // TODO: Update client_table
+        // Send a prepare message to all the replicas.
+        let view_number = self.view_number;
+        let commit_number = self.commit_number();
+        self.send_msg_to_others(Message::Prepare {
+            view_number,
+            op,
+            op_number,
+            commit_number,
+        });
+    }
+
+    /// The primary sends a `Prepare` message to replicate an operation to backup
+    /// nodes. The nodes that receive a `Prepare` message will reply with `PrepareOk`
+    /// when they have appended `op` to their logs. The message also contains the
+    /// commit number of the primary, so that the backups can commit their logs up
+    /// to that point.
+    fn on_prepare(
+        &self,
+        view_number: ViewNumber,
+        op_number: OpNumber,
+        op: SM::Input,
+        commit_number: CommitID,
+    ) {
+        assert!(!self.is_primary());
+        // Replicas process normal-case messages only when their status is
+        // normal. While we wait for `NewState`, a delayed `Prepare` must not
+        // be appended, or the log moves past the point we asked to be
+        // repaired from and the `NewState` reply no longer fits.
+        if *self.status.borrow() != Status::Normal {
+            return;
+        }
+        // TODO: If view number is not the same, initiate recovery.
+        assert_eq!(self.view_number, view_number);
+        // If we fell behind in the log, initiate state transfer.
+        if op_number > self.op_number() + 1 {
+            self.state_transfer();
+            return;
+        }
+        if op_number == self.op_number() + 1 {
+            // Append op to our log.
+            self.append_to_log(op);
+        }
+        // Otherwise we already have the op: the primary re-sent it because
+        // it has not seen our `PrepareOk`, so acknowledge it again below.
+        //
+        // Commit the log up to the commit number received in `Prepare`
+        // message, which represents the committed state of the primary. A
+        // re-sent `Prepare` can carry a commit number beyond our log; the
+        // re-sent ops that follow it close that gap.
+        let commit_number = commit_number.min(self.op_number());
+        for op_idx in self.commit_number()..commit_number {
+            self.commit_op(op_idx);
+        }
+        // Acknowledge to the primary that we have every op up to our op
+        // number.
+        self.send_msg_to_primary(Message::PrepareOk {
+            view_number,
+            op_number: self.op_number(),
+        });
+    }
+
+    /// Backup nodes send `PrepareOk` message to the primary to acknowledge that
+    /// they have appended an op to their logs. When the primary has
+    /// received `PrepareOk` messages from a quorum of replicas, it commits
+    /// the operation and replies to the client.
+    fn on_prepare_ok(&self, view_number: ViewNumber, op_number: OpNumber) {
+        assert!(self.is_primary());
+        assert_eq!(self.view_number, view_number);
+        if op_number <= self.commit_number() {
+            return; // already committed
+        }
+        // Register the acknowledgement
+        let mut acks = self.acks.borrow_mut();
+        let Some(count) = acks.get_mut(&op_number) else {
+            return;
+        };
+        *count += 1;
+        if *count != self.config.quorum() {
+            return;
+        }
+        // A quorum for `op_number` means the operation and all earlier ones
+        // are committed: backups only acknowledge an operation once they
+        // have every earlier one in their log. Earlier operations may not
+        // have reached a quorum on their own, for example because their
+        // `PrepareOk` messages were lost or overtaken, so commit everything
+        // up to `op_number`, in order, and reply to the client for each.
+        for op_idx in self.commit_number()..op_number {
+            let response = self.commit_op(op_idx);
+            self.respond_to_client(response);
+        }
+        acks.retain(|acked_op_number, _| *acked_op_number > op_number);
+    }
+
+    /// A backup node typically commits its log as part of `Prepare`
+    /// message handling because the primary uses that also to signal the
+    /// current commit number. However, `Prepare` is sent only in
+    /// reaction to a client `Request` message. If there are no client
+    /// requests, then the primary sends a `Commit` message to backup
+    /// nodes instead to give backup nodes the chance to commit.
+    fn on_commit(&self, view_number: ViewNumber, commit_number: CommitID) {
+        if *self.status.borrow() != Status::Normal {
+            return;
+        }
+        if view_number < self.view_number {
+            return;
+        }
+        assert_eq!(*self.status.borrow(), Status::Normal);
+        assert_eq!(self.view_number, view_number);
+        if commit_number > self.op_number() {
+            self.state_transfer();
+            return;
+        }
+        for op_idx in self.commit_number()..commit_number {
+            self.commit_op(op_idx);
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// A replica sends a `GetState` message to another replica to catch
+    /// up on its log.
+    fn on_get_state(&self, replica_id: ReplicaID, view_number: ViewNumber, op_number: OpNumber) {
+        assert_eq!(*self.status.borrow(), Status::Normal);
+        assert_eq!(self.view_number, view_number);
+        let log = self.log.borrow();
+        self.send_msg(
+            replica_id,
+            Message::NewState {
+                view_number: self.view_number,
+                log: log[op_number..].to_vec(),
+                op_number_start: op_number,
+                op_number_end: self.op_number(),
+                commit_number: self.commit_number(),
+            },
+        );
+    }
+
+    /// A replica receives a `NewState` message in response to a
+    /// `GetState` message it sent itself to catch up on its log.
+    fn on_new_state(
+        &self,
+        view_number: ViewNumber,
+        log: Vec<SM::Input>,
+        op_number_start: OpNumber,
+        op_number_end: OpNumber,
+        commit_number: CommitID,
+    ) {
+        if *self.status.borrow() != Status::Recovery {
+            return;
+        }
+        assert_eq!(self.view_number, view_number);
+        assert_eq!(log.len(), op_number_end - op_number_start);
+        // The reply may answer an earlier `GetState` that the network
+        // delayed or replayed, in which case it starts before our current
+        // op number. We can still use whatever it has beyond our log, since
+        // in a single view the overlapping entries are identical. A reply
+        // that starts past our log or that ends inside it is of no use, so
+        // keep waiting for another one.
+        let op_number = self.op_number();
+        if op_number_start > op_number || op_number_end <= op_number {
+            return;
+        }
+        for op in log.into_iter().skip(op_number - op_number_start) {
+            self.append_to_log(op);
+        }
+        for op_idx in self.commit_number()..commit_number {
+            self.commit_op(op_idx);
+        }
+        assert_eq!(self.op_number(), op_number_end);
+        assert!(self.commit_number() >= commit_number);
+        self.status.replace(Status::Normal);
+        let view_number = self.view_number;
+        self.send_msg_to_primary(Message::PrepareOk {
+            view_number,
+            op_number: op_number_end,
+        });
+    }
+
+    /// When there are no client requests, the primary node sends a
+    /// `Commit` message to backup nodes periodically to let them commit
+    /// if needed.
+    ///
+    /// Idle periods also drive retransmission, which the paper leaves out of
+    /// its description: a backup waiting for `NewState` asks again in case
+    /// its `GetState` or the reply was lost, and the primary re-sends the
+    /// `Prepare` for every op that has not committed yet in case a `Prepare`
+    /// or its `PrepareOk` was lost.
+    pub fn on_idle(&self) {
+        if !self.is_primary() {
+            if *self.status.borrow() == Status::Recovery {
+                self.state_transfer();
+            }
+            return;
+        }
+        assert_eq!(*self.status.borrow(), Status::Normal);
+        let view_number = self.view_number;
+        let commit_number = self.commit_number();
+        self.send_msg_to_others(Message::Commit {
+            view_number,
+            commit_number,
+        });
+        let log = self.log.borrow();
+        for op_number in commit_number + 1..=self.op_number() {
+            self.send_msg_to_others(Message::Prepare {
+                view_number,
+                op_number,
+                op: log[op_number - 1].clone(),
+                commit_number,
+            });
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn append_to_log(&self, op: SM::Input) {
+        let mut log = self.log.borrow_mut();
+        log.push(op);
+        self.op_number.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);
+    }
+
+    fn state_transfer(&self) {
+        self.status.replace(Status::Recovery);
+        // FIXME: pick *one* replica, doesn't need to be primary.
+        let primary_id = self.primary_id();
+        self.send_msg(
+            primary_id,
+            Message::GetState {
+                replica_id: self.self_id,
+                view_number: self.view_number,
+                op_number: self.op_number(),
+            },
+        );
+    }
+
+    /// Commits an operation at log index `op_idx`.
+    fn commit_op(&self, op_idx: usize) -> SM::Output {
+        let log = self.log.borrow();
+        let op = &log[op_idx];
+        let ret = self.state_machine.apply(op.clone());
+        self.commit_number.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);
+        ret
+    }
+
+    /// Sends a message to the primary.
+    fn send_msg_to_primary(&self, message: Message<SM::Input>) {
+        let primary_id = self.primary_id();
+        self.send_msg(primary_id, message);
+    }
+
+    /// Sends a message to all other replicas.
+    fn send_msg_to_others(&self, message: Message<SM::Input>) {
+        let replicas = self.config.replicas.borrow();
+        for replica_id in replicas.iter() {
+            if *replica_id == self.self_id {
+                continue;
+            }
+            self.send_msg(*replica_id, message.clone());
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn send_msg(&self, replica_id: ReplicaID, message: Message<SM::Input>) {
+        self.replica_tx.send((replica_id, message)).unwrap();
+    }
+
+    fn respond_to_client(&self, response: SM::Output) {
+        self.client_tx.send(response).unwrap();
+    }
+
+    /// Returns the ID of this replica.
+    pub fn id(&self) -> ReplicaID {
+        self.self_id
+    }
+
+    /// Returns `true` if this replica is the primary of its current view.
+    pub fn is_primary(&self) -> bool {
+        self.self_id == self.primary_id()
+    }
+
+    /// Returns the ID of the primary of the current view.
+    pub fn primary_id(&self) -> ReplicaID {
+        self.config.primary_id(self.view_number)
+    }
+
+    /// Returns the current view number.
+    pub fn view_number(&self) -> ViewNumber {
+        self.view_number
+    }
+
+    /// Returns the commit number, i.e. the number of log entries that have
+    /// been applied to the state machine.
+    pub fn commit_number(&self) -> CommitID {
+        self.commit_number.load(Ordering::SeqCst)
+    }
+
+    /// Returns the op number, i.e. the number of entries in the log.
+    pub fn op_number(&self) -> OpNumber {
+        self.op_number.load(Ordering::SeqCst)
+    }
+
+    /// Returns a read-only view of the log.
+    pub fn log(&self) -> Ref<'_, Vec<SM::Input>> {
+        self.log.borrow()
+    }
+}

COMMIT 642b3dc988d2906d24eba4119fa23e8eaafb3119
Add a durability property to the simulator

Every op the primary has committed must be in the log of a majority of
replicas. The primary commits on a quorum of PrepareOk messages and a
backup only acknowledges an op once it is in its log, so this must hold
at the tick the commit happens, and since logs are never truncated each
committed index needs checking once.

This fails today on every cluster of four or more replicas with packet
replay, for example:

    SEED=9 cargo run --release -p vsr-simulator

fails at tick 30 with the op at index 0 committed while only 2 of 4
replicas hold it. The primary counts PrepareOk messages rather than
tracking which replica sent them, so a replayed ack from one backup
counts twice and completes a quorum on its own. With three replicas the
quorum is two, which one real ack always satisfies, so it does not show.

FILE simulator/properties.rs
@@ -39,10 +39,48 @@ pub fn default_properties() -> Vec<Box<dyn Property>> {
         Box::new(CommittedPrefixAgreement::default()),
         Box::new(NoDuplicateOps::default()),
         Box::new(ResponsesMatchCommits::default()),
+        Box::new(Durability::default()),
         Box::new(Convergence),
     ]
 }
 
+/// Every op the primary has committed is in the log of a majority of
+/// replicas. The primary only commits on a quorum of `PrepareOk` messages,
+/// and a backup only acknowledges an op once it is in its log, so this must
+/// hold at the tick the commit happens. Logs are never truncated, so each
+/// committed index needs checking once.
+#[derive(Default)]
+pub struct Durability {
+    /// Number of committed entries already verified.
+    verified: usize,
+}
+
+impl Property for Durability {
+    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {
+        "durability"
+    }
+
+    fn check(&mut self, ctx: &SimContext) -> Result<()> {
+        let commit = ctx.replicas[ctx.primary_id].commit_number();
+        let quorum = ctx.replicas.len() / 2 + 1;
+        for i in self.verified..commit {
+            let copies = ctx
+                .replicas
+                .iter()
+                .filter(|replica| replica.op_number() > i)
+                .count();
+            ensure!(
+                copies >= quorum,
+                "tick {}: op at index {i} committed with {copies} of {} replicas holding it, quorum is {quorum}",
+                ctx.tick,
+                ctx.replicas.len()
+            );
+        }
+        self.verified = commit;
+        Ok(())
+    }
+}
+
 /// A replica's commit number never decreases and never exceeds its op number,
 /// and its op number always equals its log length.
 #[derive(Default)]

COMMIT 4e4b0bb60a7628b56079379b87ee61b345efd4bf
Count PrepareOk acknowledgements per replica, not per message

The primary kept a plain counter of PrepareOk messages per op and
committed when the counter reached the quorum. Two acknowledgements
from the same backup therefore looked like two backups. That happens
routinely: the network can replay a PrepareOk, and since idle periods
re-send uncommitted Prepares, a backup that already has the op
acknowledges it again. With four or more replicas the primary could
reach the quorum with its own entry plus one backup counted twice, and
commit an op that only two replicas held. A view change could then
lose that op even though the client had a reply for it.

The paper's PrepareOk carries the sender's id for this reason. Add
replica_id to the message and keep, for each uncommitted op, the set of
replicas that acknowledged it; commit when the set reaches the quorum.
With three replicas the quorum is two, which one real backup always
satisfies, so the bug never showed at that size.

Add a cluster test with four replicas where only one backup gets the
Prepare and its PrepareOk arrives twice: the primary must not commit
until the re-sent Prepare reaches another backup.

Found by the durability property added in 642b3dc, with:

    SEED=9 cargo run --release -p vsr-simulator

which failed at tick 30 with the op at index 0 committed while 2 of 4
replicas held it. With this fix that seed and every other swarm seed
tried, up to seven replicas, pass both phases.

+/// Four replicas, so a quorum is three. Only one backup receives the
+/// `Prepare`, and the network delivers its `PrepareOk` twice. Two acks from
+/// the same backup are still one backup, so the primary must not commit
+/// until another backup acknowledges.
+#[test]
+fn test_duplicate_prepare_ok_is_not_a_quorum() {
+    let _ = env_logger::try_init();
+    let (client_tx, _client_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let (replica_tx, replica_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let config = Arc::new(Config::new());
+    let sms: Vec<_> = (0..4).map(|_| Arc::new(Accumulator::new())).collect();
+    let replicas: Vec<_> = sms
+        .iter()
+        .map(|sm| {
+            let id = config.add_replica();
+            Replica::new(
+                id,
+                config.clone(),
+                sm.clone(),
+                client_tx.clone(),
+                replica_tx.clone(),
+            )
+        })
+        .collect();
+    let idle = || {
+        for replica in &replicas {
+            replica.on_idle();
+        }
+    };
+    let client = Client::new(config, replica_tx);
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(10), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    // Only replica 3 gets the Prepare, and its PrepareOk arrives twice.
+    while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+        let (replica_id, message) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+        match message {
+            Message::Prepare { .. } if replica_id != 3 => continue,
+            Message::PrepareOk { .. } => {
+                replicas[replica_id].on_message(message.clone());
+                replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+            }
+            _ => replicas[replica_id].on_message(message),
+        }
+    }
+    assert_eq!(1, replicas[3].op_number());
+    assert_eq!(0, replicas[1].op_number());
+    assert_eq!(0, replicas[2].op_number());
+    assert_eq!(0, replicas[0].commit_number());
+    // Once the other backups get the re-sent Prepare, it commits.
+    idle();
+    while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+        let (replica_id, message) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+        replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+    }
+    assert_eq!(1, replicas[0].commit_number());
+    idle();
+    while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+        let (replica_id, message) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+        replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+    }
+    for sm in &sms {
+        assert_eq!(10, *sm.accumulator.lock());
+    }
+}
+

     Add(i32),

COMMIT dd5bbb8f5252137aac1fad00f575683b2692a694
Add the client table so a re-sent request is not executed twice

The primary appended every Request it received to the log, so a request
that reached it twice, because the client re-sent it or the network
replayed it, ran twice. The paper's client table (section 4.1, step 2)
records each client's latest request number and, once executed, the
result. A request numbered at or below that is a re-send: if it is the
latest request and has executed, the primary re-sends the reply from
the table, otherwise it drops the request and the reply follows when
the original commits.

To maintain the table on backups too, which a view change will need,
log entries now carry the client id and request number along with the
op, and so do Prepare messages and the log in NewState. Replies are a
message carrying the client id, request number, and result, since a
client must be able to tell which request a reply answers and discard
duplicates. StateMachine::Output must therefore be Clone. Client::new
takes a client id instead of hardcoding 0, and on_request returns the
request number so the caller can match the reply.

The simulator tracks the outstanding request number per client and
counts a reply as progress only for that request. The property that
checked one reply per commit now checks that every reply is for a
committed request and carries the right result, allowing duplicates,
and at the end that every committed request was replied to. Client
messages are still exempt from network faults; enabling them is next.

Add a cluster test where the same request reaches the primary twice
while it is being prepared, and once more after it committed.

+/// The same client request reaches the primary twice: once more while it
+/// is still being prepared, and once more after it has committed. The
+/// primary must execute it once. It drops the duplicate that arrives while
+/// the request is in progress, and answers the one that arrives after the
+/// commit by re-sending the reply it already has.
+#[test]
+fn test_duplicate_request_executes_once() {
+    let _ = env_logger::try_init();
+    let (client_tx, client_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let (replica_tx, replica_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let config = Arc::new(Config::new());
+    let sms: Vec<_> = (0..3).map(|_| Arc::new(Accumulator::new())).collect();
+    let replicas: Vec<_> = sms
+        .iter()
+        .map(|sm| {
+            let id = config.add_replica();
+            Replica::new(
+                id,
+                config.clone(),
+                sm.clone(),
+                client_tx.clone(),
+                replica_tx.clone(),
+            )
+        })
+        .collect();
+    let tick = || {
+        while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+            let (replica_id, message) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+            replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+        }
+    };
+    let client = Client::new(0, config, replica_tx);
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(10), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    // The request is delivered twice before anything else happens.
+    let (replica_id, request) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+    replicas[replica_id].on_message(request.clone());
+    replicas[replica_id].on_message(request.clone());
+    tick();
+    assert_eq!(1, replicas[0].op_number());
+    assert_eq!(1, replicas[0].commit_number());
+    assert_eq!(10, *sms[0].accumulator.lock());
+    assert_eq!(1, client_rx.try_iter().count());
+    // The request is delivered once more after it committed: the primary
+    // re-sends the reply without running it again.
+    replicas[replica_id].on_message(request);
+    tick();
+    assert_eq!(1, replicas[0].op_number());
+    assert_eq!(10, *sms[0].accumulator.lock());
+    assert_eq!(1, client_rx.try_iter().count());
+}

COMMIT bbcc14ddcc9a910605d193980529ec628610ed91
Re-send a client request that got no reply, and fault client messages

A client sent its request once. If the network lost it, nothing ever
re-sent it, so the request was gone for good and the simulator had to
keep client messages exempt from faults.

Following the paper (section 4.1), the client keeps its pending request
and on an idle period re-sends it to every replica, since the primary
may have changed without the client knowing. Backups ignore client
requests instead of asserting they are the primary. The client table
added in dd5bbb8 makes the re-sends safe: the primary drops a duplicate
of a request in progress and answers one that already committed from
the table.

The simulator now runs the clients' idle logic on the heartbeat
interval alongside the replicas', and Options::swarm faults client
messages for half the seeds. The --fault-client-messages flag forces
it on. Every seed tried with client loss and replay passes both phases.

Add a cluster test where the request is lost, the client re-sends it
on idle, and after the reply it stops re-sending.

FILE tests/cluster.rs
@@ -652,6 +652,55 @@ fn test_duplicate_request_executes_once() {
     assert_eq!(1, client_rx.try_iter().count());
 }
 
+/// The client's request is lost. On its next idle period the client must
+/// re-send it, to every replica since the primary may have changed, and
+/// backups must ignore it.
+#[test]
+fn test_lost_request_resent_on_idle() {
+    let _ = env_logger::try_init();
+    let (client_tx, client_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let (replica_tx, replica_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let config = Arc::new(Config::new());
+    let sms: Vec<_> = (0..3).map(|_| Arc::new(Accumulator::new())).collect();
+    let replicas: Vec<_> = sms
+        .iter()
+        .map(|sm| {
+            let id = config.add_replica();
+            Replica::new(
+                id,
+                config.clone(),
+                sm.clone(),
+                client_tx.clone(),
+                replica_tx.clone(),
+            )
+        })
+        .collect();
+    let tick_with = |deliver: &dyn Fn(usize, &Message<Op>) -> bool| {
+        while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+            let (replica_id, message) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+            if deliver(replica_id, &message) {
+                replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+            }
+        }
+    };
+    let client = Client::new(0, config, replica_tx);
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(10), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick_with(&|_, message| !matches!(message, Message::Request { .. }));
+    assert_eq!(0, replicas[0].op_number());
+    // The idle period must make the client re-send the request.
+    client.on_idle();
+    tick_with(&|_, _| true);
+    assert_eq!(1, replicas[0].commit_number());
+    assert_eq!(10, *sms[0].accumulator.lock());
+    assert_eq!(1, client_rx.try_iter().count());
+    // Once replied to, the request is not re-sent again.
+    client.on_reply(0);
+    client.on_idle();
+    tick_with(&|_, _| true);
+    assert_eq!(1, replicas[0].op_number());
+    assert_eq!(0, client_rx.try_iter().count());
+}
+
 #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
 enum Op {
     Add(i32),

COMMIT 5f4c413c34bbf0a72f3fda94663fa68dbc3a96d8
Crash and restart replicas in the simulator, with a liveness core

Replicas can now crash: a crashed replica receives no messages, sends
none, and does not run its idle logic, but keeps its state, since a
replica that lost its memory is the recovery protocol's job and that
does not exist yet. The per-tick crash and restart probabilities and
their stability periods are drawn from the seed like every other
option. Any replica can crash, including the primary.

The liveness phase now picks a core, a random majority of replicas,
brings it up, and crashes everything else for good, after TigerBeetle's
liveness testing: the protocol promises progress as long as a majority
is up, so the core must converge on its own. Convergence and the final
property checks consider the core only. Tests and runs that are not
about losing the primary can ask for a full core.

Without view changes, a core that excludes the primary cannot make
progress, and that is the point: this is the failing test for view
changes. For example

    SEED=5 cargo run --release -p vsr-simulator -- --lite

picks core [1, 2], crashes the primary for good, and fails with
"no state convergence: pending commit". Seeds whose core keeps the
primary still pass, crashes and all.

+    /// kept up and the rest is left as it is.
+    fn tick_crash(&mut self) {

COMMIT 06ba5def97a7ff25c157f658268ccf2be894ad23
Implement view changes

When the primary fails, the backups must agree on a new one and on the
log it starts from, without losing anything a client was told had
committed. This is the view change protocol of section 4.2 of the paper.

A backup that has not heard from the primary for Config::primary_timeout
idle periods moves to the next view, sets its status to view-change, and
sends StartViewChange to every replica. A replica that hears of a higher
view this way adopts it rather than counting up on its own, as the TLA+
analysis recommends. With StartViewChange from f other replicas, a
replica sends DoViewChange to the new view's primary, carrying its log,
its commit number, and the last view in which it was normal. With a
quorum of those, the new primary takes the log from the latest normal
view, the longest if several, which by quorum intersection holds every
committed op, commits what the votes say is committed, replies to the
clients for anything the old primary did not get to, and sends
StartView. Backups replace their log with it, commit up to the commit
number in it, and acknowledge the rest, which the new primary then
commits as usual. Idle periods re-send StartViewChange and DoViewChange,
and a view change that itself takes primary_timeout idle periods is
followed by one for the next view, in case the new primary is down too.

A replica that receives Prepare or Commit from a later view, or from
its own view while it is still changing to it, has missed the change.
It asks the new primary for the log from its commit number on, and only
when the reply arrives replaces the suffix of its log with it. This is
the state transfer fix from the TLA+ analysis: the paper truncates to
the commit number first, which can drop a committed op below a majority
if the reply never comes. Within a view, state transfer still merges
by op number as before. A StartView for a view the replica is already
normal in is ignored, so a replayed one cannot replace a newer log.
The commit number never moves backwards, and a log is only installed if
it contains the replica's committed prefix; the client table is rebuilt
from the new log, keeping the replies of committed requests.

Replies carry the view number and the client adopts it, so its next
request goes to the new primary. Every reply also lets the client
discard duplicates, and the client already re-sends to all replicas on
idle, which is how a request outlives a primary.

The simulator draws the primary timeout from the seed, bounded below by
a few times the mean network delay so that most delayed heartbeats do
not look like a dead primary, and its properties no longer assume that
replica 0 is the primary: durability is checked on whichever replica
commits, expected replies come from whichever replica has committed
furthest, and duplicate ops are checked in the committed prefix only,
since a view change may replace entries beyond it. Its own tests now
run with crashes and a random core.

Add a cluster test where the primary crashes with an op prepared on the
backups but uncommitted: the backups change view, the new primary
commits the op and replies, and the client follows it to the new view.

The failing simulator run was

    SEED=5 cargo run --release -p vsr-simulator -- --lite

at commit 5f4c413, whose core excluded the primary and which stopped
with "no state convergence: pending commit". Every seed tried passes
now, including cores without replica 0 and full runs with hundreds of
crashes and restarts, and a batch of 5815 random seeds found no
failure. One seed, 12458081382810450947, livelocks: with a heartbeat
every tick and a five tick timeout, view changes keep starting before
the previous one can complete. That needs backoff on the view change
timeout and is left for the next change.

+    /// Checks a `Prepare` or `Commit` from the primary of `view_number`
+    /// against our own view and status, and returns whether to process it
+    /// as a normal-case message.
+    ///
+    /// A message from a later view means a view change happened without
+    /// us, and one for our own view while we are still changing to it means
+    /// the view has started: either way we first catch up with the view's
+    /// state from its primary.
+    fn accept_from_primary(&self, view_number: ViewNumber) -> bool {
+        let view = self.view_number.get();
+        if view_number < view {
+            return false;
+        }
+        if view_number > view {
+            self.catch_up_with_view(view_number);
+            return false;
+        }
+        // The primary of our view is alive.
+        self.heard_from_primary.set(true);
+        match self.status.get() {
+            Status::Normal => !self.is_primary(),
+            Status::Recovery => false,
+            Status::ViewChange => {
+                self.catch_up_with_view(view_number);
+                false
+            }
+        }
+    }

+            || self.catching_up.get()
+            || self.do_view_change_sent.get()
+        {
+            return;

+    /// Sends our state to the new view's primary, or records it directly if
+    /// that is us.
+    fn send_do_view_change(&self) {
+        let view_number = self.view_number.get();
+        let vote = ViewChangeVote {
+            last_normal_view: self.last_normal_view.get(),
+            log: self.log.borrow().clone(),
+            commit_number: self.commit_number(),
+        };
+        let primary_id = self.config.primary_id(view_number);
+        if primary_id == self.self_id {
+            self.record_do_view_change(self.self_id, vote);
+        } else {
+            self.send_msg(
+                primary_id,
+                Message::DoViewChange {
+                    view_number,
+                    replica_id: self.self_id,
+                    last_normal_view: vote.last_normal_view,
+                    op_number: vote.log.len(),
+                    log: vote.log,
+                    commit_number: vote.commit_number,
+                },
+            );
+        }
+    }

+    /// Records a `DoViewChange` vote and, with a quorum of them, starts the
+    /// view: the log is the one from the latest normal view, the longest if
+    /// several, which by quorum intersection holds every committed op.
+    fn record_do_view_change(&self, replica_id: ReplicaID, vote: ViewChangeVote<SM::Input>) {
+        let mut votes = self.do_view_change_votes.borrow_mut();
+        votes.insert(replica_id, vote);
+        if votes.len() < self.config.quorum() {
+            return;
+        }
+        let best = votes
+            .values()
+            .max_by_key(|vote| (vote.last_normal_view, vote.log.len()))
+            .unwrap();
+        let log = best.log.clone();
+        let commit_number = votes.values().map(|vote| vote.commit_number).max().unwrap();
+        drop(votes);
+        trace!(
+            "Replica {} starts view {} with {} ops, {commit_number} committed",
+            self.self_id,
+            self.view_number.get(),
+            log.len()
+        );
+        self.install_log(log);
+        // Execute what committed in earlier views but was not yet executed
+        // here, and reply to the clients: the old primary may have failed
+        // before it could.
+        self.commit_up_to(commit_number, true);
+        self.enter_normal();
+        let mut acks = self.acks.borrow_mut();
+        acks.clear();
+        for op_number in self.commit_number() + 1..=self.op_number() {
+            acks.insert(op_number, HashSet::from([self.self_id]));
+        }
+        drop(acks);
+        for replica_id in self.config.replicas.borrow().iter() {
+            if *replica_id != self.self_id {
+                self.send_start_view(*replica_id);
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn send_start_view(&self, replica_id: ReplicaID) {
+        self.send_msg(
+            replica_id,
+            Message::StartView {
+                view_number: self.view_number.get(),
+                log: self.log.borrow().clone(),
+                op_number: self.op_number(),
+                commit_number: self.commit_number(),
+            },
+        );
+    }

+    /// Learns that `view_number` has started, and asks its primary for the
+    /// log from our commit number on.
+    fn catch_up_with_view(&self, view_number: ViewNumber) {
+        if self.view_number.get() == view_number && self.catching_up.get() {
+            return;
+        }
+        trace!(
+            "Replica {} catches up with view {view_number}",
+            self.self_id
+        );
+        self.view_number.set(view_number);
+        self.status.set(Status::ViewChange);
+        self.catching_up.set(true);
+        self.idle_periods_waiting.set(0);
+        self.clear_view_change_state();
+        self.send_get_state(self.commit_number());
+    }

+    fn enter_normal(&self) {
+        self.status.set(Status::Normal);
+        self.last_normal_view.set(self.view_number.get());
+        self.catching_up.set(false);
+        self.heard_from_primary.set(true);
+        self.idle_periods_waiting.set(0);
+        self.clear_view_change_state();

+    /// Replaces the log with `log`, which must contain our committed prefix,
+    /// and rebuilds the client table from it. Replies kept for committed
+    /// requests survive; the rest of the table follows the new log.
+    fn install_log(&self, log: Vec<LogEntry<SM::Input>>) {
+        let commit_number = self.commit_number();
+        assert!(log.len() >= commit_number);
+        let mut client_table = self.client_table.borrow_mut();
+        let old_table = std::mem::take(&mut *client_table);
+        for (i, entry) in log.iter().enumerate() {
+            let reply = if i < commit_number {
+                old_table
+                    .get(&entry.client_id)
+                    .filter(|old| old.request_number == entry.request_number)
+                    .and_then(|old| old.reply.clone())
+            } else {
+                None
+            };
+            client_table.insert(
+                entry.client_id,
+                ClientEntry {
+                    request_number: entry.request_number,
+                    reply,
+                },
+            );
+        }
+        self.op_number.store(log.len(), Ordering::SeqCst);
+        *self.log.borrow_mut() = log;
+    }
+

+    /// within the current view.
     fn state_transfer(&self) {

+        self.send_get_state(self.op_number());
+    }
+
+    fn send_get_state(&self, op_number: OpNumber) {
+        self.send_msg_to_primary(Message::GetState {
+            replica_id: self.self_id,
+            view_number: self.view_number.get(),
+            op_number,
+        });
+    }
+
+    /// Acknowledges to the primary that we have every op up to our op
+    /// number.
+    fn send_prepare_ok(&self) {
+        self.send_msg_to_primary(Message::PrepareOk {
+            view_number: self.view_number.get(),
+            op_number: self.op_number(),
+            replica_id: self.self_id,
+        });
+    }
+
+    /// Commits every op up to `commit_number`, in order, replying to the
+    /// clients if `reply` is set. The commit number never moves backwards.
+    fn commit_up_to(&self, commit_number: CommitID, reply: bool) {
+        for op_idx in self.commit_number()..commit_number {
+            let response = self.commit_op(op_idx);
+            if reply {
+                self.respond_to_client(response);
+            }
+        }
     }

+/// The primary crashes with an op prepared on the backups but not yet
+/// committed. The backups must notice the silence, move to view 1 with
+/// replica 1 as primary, and the new primary must commit the op it found
+/// in the logs and reply to the client. The client learns the new view from
+/// the reply and sends its next request to the new primary.
+#[test]
+fn test_view_change_after_primary_crash() {
+    let _ = env_logger::try_init();
+    let (client_tx, client_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let (replica_tx, replica_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let config = Arc::new(Config::new());
+    let sms: Vec<_> = (0..3).map(|_| Arc::new(Accumulator::new())).collect();
+    let replicas: Vec<_> = sms
+        .iter()
+        .map(|sm| {
+            let id = config.add_replica();
+            Replica::new(
+                id,
+                config.clone(),
+                sm.clone(),
+                client_tx.clone(),
+                replica_tx.clone(),
+            )
+        })
+        .collect();
+    let tick_with = |deliver: &dyn Fn(usize, &Message<Op>) -> bool| {
+        while !replica_rx.is_empty() {
+            let (replica_id, message) = replica_rx.recv().unwrap();
+            if deliver(replica_id, &message) {
+                replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+            }
+        }
+    };
+    let idle_all = || {
+        for replica in &replicas {
+            replica.on_idle();
+        }
+    };
+    // Replica 0 has crashed: it gets nothing and runs no idle logic.
+    let idle_without_0 = || {
+        replicas[1].on_idle();
+        replicas[2].on_idle();
+    };
+    let client = Client::new(0, config, replica_tx);
+
+    // Two ops commit everywhere in view 0.
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(10), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(20), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick_with(&|_, _| true);
+    idle_all();
+    tick_with(&|_, _| true);
+    for sm in &sms {
+        assert_eq!(30, *sm.accumulator.lock());
+    }
+    assert_eq!(2, client_rx.try_iter().count());
+
+    // A third op reaches the backups, but the primary crashes before it
+    // sees their acknowledgements.
+    let request_number = client.on_request(Op::Add(30), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick_with(&|replica_id, message| replica_id != 0 || matches!(message, Message::Request { .. }));
+    assert_eq!(3, replicas[1].op_number());
+    assert_eq!(3, replicas[2].op_number());
+    assert_eq!(2, replicas[0].commit_number());
+
+    // The backups stop hearing from the primary and change view.
+    for _ in 0..10 {
+        idle_without_0();
+        tick_with(&|replica_id, _| replica_id != 0);
+    }
+    assert_eq!(1, replicas[1].view_number());
+    assert_eq!(1, replicas[2].view_number());
+    assert!(replicas[1].is_primary());
+    assert!(!replicas[2].is_primary());
+    // The new primary commits the op it found and replies to the client.
+    assert_eq!(3, replicas[1].commit_number());
+    assert_eq!(3, replicas[2].commit_number());
+    assert_eq!(60, *sms[1].accumulator.lock());
+    assert_eq!(60, *sms[2].accumulator.lock());
+    let replies: Vec<_> = client_rx.try_iter().collect();
+    assert_eq!(1, replies.len());
+    assert_eq!(request_number, replies[0].request_number);
+    assert_eq!(1, replies[0].view_number);
+
+    // The client learns the view from the reply and sends the next request
+    // to the new primary.
+    client.on_reply(replies[0].request_number, replies[0].view_number);
+    client.on_request(Op::Add(40), Box::new(|_| {}));
+    tick_with(&|replica_id, _| replica_id != 0);
+    idle_without_0();
+    tick_with(&|replica_id, _| replica_id != 0);
+    assert_eq!(4, replicas[1].commit_number());
+    assert_eq!(4, replicas[2].commit_number());
+    assert_eq!(100, *sms[1].accumulator.lock());
+    assert_eq!(100, *sms[2].accumulator.lock());
+    assert_eq!(1, client_rx.try_iter().count());
+    // The crashed primary never moved.
+    assert_eq!(0, replicas[0].view_number());
+    assert_eq!(30, *sms[0].accumulator.lock());
+}
+
 #[derive(Clone, Debug)]
 enum Op {
     Add(i32),

COMMIT 8ab4fffc90c52c153433389c496f7a0dd6afeba0
Back off the view change timeout after each failed view change

A replica waited the same primary timeout for every view change. When
that timeout is shorter than a view change takes, which happens with
short heartbeat intervals and delayed or lost messages, some replica's
timer fires before the change completes, starts the next view, and
every other replica adopts it and restarts its own timer. Nothing ever
completes and the cluster is livelocked.

Each view change entered while already in a view change, whether on the
replica's own timer or by adopting a view another replica started, now
doubles the wait before the next one, capped at 2^10 times the base
timeout. Returning to normal status resets it. The wait soon exceeds
the time a view change takes, one completes, and normal operation
resumes. The paper only says that liveness depends on setting timeouts
properly; this is how TigerBeetle sets them.

Add a cluster test that cuts a backup off and checks that the idle
periods between the view changes it starts are one, two, and four
timeouts.

Found by the simulator at commit 06ba5de with

    SEED=12458081382810450947 cargo run --release -p vsr-simulator

which stalled at 1739 of 10000 requests for a million ticks: a
heartbeat every tick, a five tick timeout, six replicas, and after a
crash the views advanced forever. With this change the seed passes in
61342 ticks.

+/// A backup that cannot reach anyone keeps starting view changes. The first
+/// waits the primary timeout, and each one after that waits twice as long
+/// as the last, otherwise replicas whose timers fire faster than a view
+/// change can complete keep interrupting each other forever.
+#[test]
+fn test_view_change_timeout_backs_off() {
+    let _ = env_logger::try_init();
+    let (client_tx, _client_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let (replica_tx, replica_rx) = crossbeam_channel::unbounded();
+    let config = Arc::new(Config::new());
+    let sms: Vec<_> = (0..3).map(|_| Arc::new(Accumulator::new())).collect();
+    let replicas: Vec<_> = sms
+        .iter()
+        .map(|sm| {
+            let id = config.add_replica();
+            Replica::new(
+                id,
+                config.clone(),
+                sm.clone(),
+                client_tx.clone(),
+                replica_tx.clone(),
+            )
+        })
+        .collect();
+    // Replica 1 is alone: nothing it sends is delivered. Record the idle
+    // period at which it first asks for each new view.
+    let mut first_asked_at: Vec<usize> = Vec::new();
+    for idle_period in 1..=200 {
+        replicas[1].on_idle();
+        for (_, message) in replica_rx.try_iter() {
+            if let Message::StartViewChange { view_number, .. } = message {
+                if view_number > first_asked_at.len() {
+                    first_asked_at.push(idle_period);
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        if first_asked_at.len() == 4 {
+            break;
+        }
+    }
+    assert_eq!(
+        4,
+        first_asked_at.len(),
+        "asked for views at {first_asked_at:?}"
+    );
+    let timeout = config.primary_timeout();
+    let gaps: Vec<usize> = first_asked_at.windows(2).map(|w| w[1] - w[0]).collect();
+    assert_eq!(
+        vec![timeout, 2 * timeout, 4 * timeout],
+        gaps,
+        "asked for views at {first_asked_at:?}"
+    );
+}
+

     Add(i32),

COMMIT f9a6d4ea814b59b81325cdbd5d1ed4d0a73aea02
Step the replica and client instead of handing them channels

The library did no I/O, but it still owned two channel senders that
every handler pushed into: one for messages to other replicas, one for
replies to clients. That made crossbeam-channel a dependency, put its
Sender type in the public API, and forced the replica's state behind
Cell, RefCell, and atomics so handlers could take &self. It also hid
what a step produced.

Follow TigerBeetle's shape, and Betelgeuse's: a Replica and a Client
are state machines their owner steps. on_message, on_reply, and on_idle
take &mut self; what they want sent accumulates in an outbox that the
owner drains afterwards with drain_messages, drain_replies, and drain,
and delivers however it likes: over sockets, through a simulated
network, or straight into another replica in a test. The replica owns
its state machine, whose apply now takes &mut self, and exposes it for
inspection. Config is a plain value each replica and client holds a
copy of. The client callback is gone; on_reply returns whether the
reply answered the pending request. The library depends on log alone.

The simulator hands outboxes to its network after every step, delivers
replies to the clients directly, and drops its channel dependency too.
Its network gained a send method in place of draining a receiver. The
cluster tests gained a small Cluster helper that queues what replicas
and the client produce and delivers it under the test's control, which
replaces the setup every test repeated. The kvstore example keeps its
threads for sockets and timers, using std::sync::mpsc to feed the one
thread that owns the replica, and flushes the outboxes after every
event.

Protocol code is unchanged apart from the mechanics; all seeds tried
pass as before and clippy is clean across the workspace, since the Arc
around Config that it kept flagging is gone.

FILE src/lib.rs
@@ -2,14 +2,18 @@
 //!
 //! A work-in-progress implementation of the protocol described in
 //! "Viewstamped Replication Revisited" by Liskov and Cowling.
+//!
+//! The library does no I/O, keeps no clocks, and starts no threads. A
+//! [`Replica`] and a [`Client`] are state machines that their owner steps:
+//! hand them incoming messages with `on_message` and `on_reply`, tell them
+//! time has passed with `on_idle`, and afterwards drain what they want sent
+//! with `drain_messages`, `drain_replies`, and `drain`. The owner decides
+//! how those get delivered, whether over sockets, through a simulated
+//! network, or straight into another replica in a test.
