+
+/// Replicas and one client, with the messages between them held in a queue
+/// that the test delivers as it sees fit.
+struct Cluster {
+    replicas: Vec<Replica<Accumulator>>,
+    client: Client<Op>,
+    queue: VecDeque<(ReplicaID, Message<Op>)>,
+    replies: Vec<Reply<()>>,
+}
+
+impl Cluster {
+    fn new(replica_count: usize) -> Cluster {
+        let _ = env_logger::try_init();
+        let mut config = Config::new();
+        for _ in 0..replica_count {
+            config.add_replica();
+        }
+        let replicas = (0..replica_count)
+            .map(|id| Replica::new(id, config.clone(), Accumulator::default()))
+            .collect();
+        Cluster {
+            replicas,
+            client: Client::new(0, config),
+            queue: VecDeque::new(),
+            replies: Vec::new(),
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn request(&mut self, op: Op) -> RequestNumber {
+        self.client.on_request(op)
+    }
+
+    /// Moves everything the replicas and the client want sent into the
+    /// queue, and collects the replies.
+    fn collect(&mut self) {
+        for replica in &mut self.replicas {
+            self.queue.extend(replica.drain_messages());
+            self.replies.extend(replica.drain_replies());
         }
-        for replica in &replicas {
+        self.queue.extend(self.client.drain());
+    }
+
+    /// Delivers every queued message for which `deliver` returns true, and
+    /// whatever those deliveries produce, until nothing is left.
+    fn tick_with(&mut self, deliver: &dyn Fn(ReplicaID, &Message<Op>) -> bool) {
+        loop {
+            self.collect();
+            if self.queue.is_empty() {
+                return;
+            }
+            for (replica_id, message) in std::mem::take(&mut self.queue) {
+                if deliver(replica_id, &message) {
+                    self.replicas[replica_id].on_message(message);
+                }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn tick(&mut self) {
+        self.tick_with(&|_, _| true);
+    }
+
+    /// Delivers everything except messages to `dead`.
+    fn tick_without(&mut self, dead: ReplicaID) {
+        self.tick_with(&|replica_id, _| replica_id != dead);
+    }
+
+    fn idle(&mut self) {
+        for replica in &mut self.replicas {
             replica.on_idle();
         }
-    };
-    let client = Client::new(0, config, replica_tx);
-    client.on_request(Op::Add(10), Box::new(|_| {}));
-    tick();
-    let accumulator = (sm.accumulator.lock().unwrap()).to_owned();
-    assert_eq!(10, accumulator);
-    client.on_request(Op::Sub(5), Box::new(|_| {}));
-    tick();
-    let accumulator = (sm.accumulator.lock().unwrap()).to_owned();
-    assert_eq!(5, accumulator);
-    tick();
-    let accumulator_b = (sm.accumulator.lock().unwrap()).to_owned();
-    let accumulator_c = (sm.accumulator.lock().unwrap()).to_owned();
-    assert_eq!(accumulator, accumulator_b);
-    assert_eq!(accumulator, accumulator_c);
+    }
+
+    fn take_replies(&mut self) -> Vec<Reply<()>> {
+        self.collect();
+        std::mem::take(&mut self.replies)
+    }
+
+    fn value(&self, replica_id: ReplicaID) -> i32 {
+        self.replicas[replica_id].state_machine().value
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn test_normal_operation() {
+    let mut cluster = Cluster::new(3);
+    cluster.request(Op::Add(10));
+    cluster.tick();
+    assert_eq!(10, cluster.value(0));
+    cluster.request(Op::Sub(5));
+    cluster.tick();
+    assert_eq!(5, cluster.value(0));
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn test_idle() {
+    let mut cluster = Cluster::new(3);
+    cluster.request(Op::Add(10));
+    cluster.tick();
+    cluster.idle();
+    cluster.tick();
+    assert_eq!(10, cluster.value(0));
+    cluster.request(Op::Sub(5));
+    cluster.tick();
+    cluster.idle();
+    cluster.tick();
+    for id in 0..3 {
+        assert_eq!(5, cluster.value(id));
+    }
 }

COMMIT 949b3d7f5c84e4e02998b91d3c9a3a86e2b2df68
Simulate real crashes: a restarted replica may come back with no memory

Until now a crashed replica kept its state and a restart resumed it, as
if it had only been paused. That is not what a crash does to a replica
that keeps everything in memory. A restart now has a per-seed
probability of being a reboot: the replica is replaced by a fresh one
with an empty log, view 0, and no memory of what it acknowledged or
which views it took part in. The properties that track state per
replica start over for a rebooted one.

This is the failing test for the recovery protocol of section 4.3 of
the paper, which the library does not implement: a rebooted replica
rejoins as a normal backup, or worse, as the primary of view 0. In a
30 second batch, 382 of 5695 seeds failed, in three ways:

- A replica that had committed ops received a log without them. The
  library's own assertion that an installed log holds the committed
  prefix fires, for example with

      cargo run --release -p vsr-simulator -- --lite 10677592078291100242

  at tick 43726. That is committed data lost in a view change after
  a replica that had acknowledged it forgot.

- Durability: a replica committed an op that fewer than a quorum of
  replicas hold, for example

      cargo run --release -p vsr-simulator -- 10267825576565313013

  at tick 190613 with 2 of 4 replicas holding the op.

- Two replicas committed different ops at the same index. A rebooted
  replica 0 believes it is the primary of view 0 in normal status,
  accepts re-sent client requests, and another rebooted replica
  acknowledges them, so view 0 commits a second history next to the
  live view's. That is the split brain the paper's section 8 warns of,
  and what recovery's "recovering" status prevents.

Also fix the replies property, which sliced the log of the replica that
had committed furthest even after that replica rebooted and its log
had shrunk.

FILE simulator/lib.rs

COMMIT f8acf515a3c1b2acee498d3b7ccef53463830a97
Implement replica recovery

A replica that crashed and lost its memory can now rejoin safely. This
is the recovery protocol of section 4.3 of the paper, with the fix that
Michael et al. showed it needs.

Replica::recover builds a replica in recovering status. It sends
Recovery, with a nonce, to every other replica, re-sends it on idle,
and until it has recovered it acts on nothing else: not Prepare, not a
view change, not a client request. Replicas in normal status answer
RecoveryResponse with their view; the primary of that view includes its
log and commit number. With a quorum of responses carrying its nonce,
including one from the primary of the latest view among them, the
recovering replica installs that primary's log, executes the committed
prefix, and returns to normal in that view.

One thing has to survive the crash: the view number. The library asks
its owner to persist Replica::view_number after every step, before
delivering what the step produced, and to pass it to recover. A
recovering replica will not rejoin a view older than the one it
persisted. Without that, as Michael et al. show, a replica can send
StartViewChange, crash, recover in the old view because nobody has
moved yet, and then its old message completes a view change it no
longer knows it started, so two views run at once. The persisted view
also travels in Recovery: a replica behind it takes that as the request
for the view change the crashed replica had started and forgotten, and
starts it, which is what the lost StartViewChange would have done.
Otherwise a replica that persisted a view nobody else ever entered
would wait forever.

The state transfer status is renamed StateTransfer so it is not
confused with recovering.

The simulator persists each replica's view before its messages go out
and rebuilds a rebooted replica with Replica::recover, drawing the
nonce from the seed. It limits reboots to fewer than f replicas
recovering at once, since a replica without its memory counts as
failed until recovered, and the liveness core is drawn so that it holds
a quorum of replicas that are not recovering, for the same reason.
Durability now counts holders among the replicas that are not
recovering, against the quorums those replicas could form. The kvstore
example keeps the view number in a file next to the binary and comes
back through recovery when the file exists.

Add a cluster test where a replica reboots, ignores an op that arrives
while it is recovering, recovers from the others' responses with the
primary's log, and takes part in the next op.

Found by the simulator at commit 949b3d7, where a rebooted replica
simply rejoined and 382 of 5695 seeds failed, for example

    cargo run --release -p vsr-simulator -- --lite 10677592078291100242

whose log installation assertion fired at tick 43726 after a view
change had lost a committed op. With this change 7051 random seeds pass
with no failure, including that one, the durability failure of seed
10267825576565313013, and the split brain of seed 10080230919357778125.

@@ -809,6 +910,99 @@ impl<SM: StateMachine> Replica<SM> {
         self.send_prepare_ok();
     }
 
+    /// A replica in normal status answers a recovering replica with its
+    /// view; if it is the primary, with its log and commit number too.
+    ///
+    /// The recovering replica persisted `view_number` before it crashed.
+    /// If that is ahead of us, it had started a view change we never heard
+    /// of, and it will not rejoin a view older than that, so start the
+    /// change now as if its `StartViewChange` had just arrived.
+    fn on_recovery(&mut self, replica_id: ReplicaID, nonce: u64, view_number: ViewNumber) {
+        if view_number > self.view_number && self.status != Status::Recovering {
+            self.start_view_change(view_number);
+            return;
+        }
+        if self.status != Status::Normal {
+            return;
+        }
+        let state = self.is_primary().then(|| RecoveryState {
+            log: self.log.clone(),
+            commit_number: self.commit_number,
+        });
+        let message = Message::RecoveryResponse {
+            view_number: self.view_number,
+            nonce,
+            replica_id: self.self_id,
+            state,
+        };
+        self.send(replica_id, message);
+    }
+
+    /// Collects recovery responses. With a quorum of them, including one
+    /// from the primary of the latest view among them, the replica takes
+    /// that primary's state and is back. The latest view must be at least
+    /// the one persisted before the crash: a lower one means the cluster
+    /// has not yet caught up with a view change this replica took part in
+    /// and then forgot, and joining it could let that view change complete
+    /// against a view already running.
+    fn on_recovery_response(
+        &mut self,
+        view_number: ViewNumber,
+        nonce: u64,
+        replica_id: ReplicaID,
+        state: Option<RecoveryState<SM::Input>>,
+    ) {
+        if self.status != Status::Recovering || nonce != self.recovery_nonce {
+            return;
+        }
+        self.recovery_responses
+            .insert(replica_id, RecoveryResponse { view_number, state });
+        if self.recovery_responses.len() < self.config.quorum() {
+            return;
+        }
+        let latest_view = self
+            .recovery_responses
+            .values()
+            .map(|response| response.view_number)
+            .max()
+            .unwrap();
+        if latest_view < self.view_number {
+            return;
+        }
+        let primary_id = self.config.primary_id(latest_view);
+        let Some(RecoveryResponse {
+            view_number: primary_view,
+            state: Some(state),
+        }) = self.recovery_responses.get(&primary_id)
+        else {
+            return;
+        };
+        if *primary_view != latest_view {
+            return;
+        }
+        let state = state.clone();
+        trace!(
+            "Replica {} recovers into view {latest_view} with {} ops, {} committed",
+            self.self_id,
+            state.log.len(),
+            state.commit_number
+        );
+        self.recovery_responses.clear();
+        self.view_number = latest_view;
+        self.install_log(state.log);
+        self.commit_up_to(state.commit_number, false);
+        self.enter_normal();
+    }
+
+    fn send_recovery(&mut self) {
+        let message = Message::Recovery {
+            replica_id: self.self_id,
+            nonce: self.recovery_nonce,
+            view_number: self.view_number,
+        };
+        self.send_to_others(message);
+    }
+
     /// When there are no client requests, the primary node sends a
     /// `Commit` message to backup nodes periodically to let them commit
     /// if needed.
@@ -817,8 +1011,9 @@ impl<SM: StateMachine> Replica<SM> {

+
+/// Replica 1 crashes and comes back with no memory. Until it has recovered
+/// it must take no part in the protocol: not acknowledge, not vote. Once
+/// the others answer its Recovery, it must hold the primary's log and
+/// state, and take part again.
+#[test]
+fn test_recovery_after_reboot() {
+    let mut cluster = Cluster::new(3);
+    cluster.request(Op::Add(10));
+    cluster.request(Op::Add(20));
+    cluster.tick();
+    cluster.idle();
+    cluster.tick();
+    for id in 0..3 {
+        assert_eq!(30, cluster.value(id));
+    }
+
+    // Replica 1 reboots. It last persisted view 0, and picks nonce 42.
+    cluster.replicas[1] =
+        Replica::recover(1, cluster.config.clone(), Accumulator::default(), 0, 42);
+    assert!(cluster.replicas[1].is_recovering());
+    assert_eq!(0, cluster.replicas[1].op_number());
+
+    // A new op arrives while it is still recovering: it must not touch it.
+    // Nothing it sent has been delivered yet, only the primary's messages.
+    cluster.request(Op::Add(30));
+    cluster.tick_with(&|_, message| !matches!(message, Message::Recovery { .. }));
+    assert!(cluster.replicas[1].is_recovering());
+    assert_eq!(0, cluster.replicas[1].op_number());
+    assert_eq!(3, cluster.replicas[0].commit_number());
+    assert_eq!(60, cluster.value(0));
+
+    // Its Recovery reaches the others, and their responses bring it back.
+    cluster.idle();
+    cluster.tick();
+    assert!(!cluster.replicas[1].is_recovering());
+    assert_eq!(3, cluster.replicas[1].op_number());
+    assert_eq!(3, cluster.replicas[1].commit_number());
+    assert_eq!(60, cluster.value(1));
+    assert_eq!(0, cluster.replicas[1].view_number());
+
+    // And it takes part in the next op.
+    cluster.request(Op::Add(40));
+    cluster.tick_with(&|replica_id, message| {
+        replica_id != 2 || matches!(message, Message::Request { .. })
+    });
+    assert_eq!(4, cluster.replicas[0].commit_number());
+    assert_eq!(100, cluster.value(0));
+    cluster.idle();
+    cluster.tick();
+    assert_eq!(100, cluster.value(1));
+}

Move src/lib.rs to the top level

The library is one file; a src directory for it is noise. Betelgeuse
and the simulator crate lay their files out the same way.

[NO API PATCH]

COMMIT cffcb6da9bd52688b74b079f76127feac46bd634
Add bounded model checking with Kani

The simulator picks message orderings at random. Kani, a model checker
for Rust, tries every ordering within a bound instead, on the library as
it is, with no changes to it.

The new verify crate holds a cluster driver and the harnesses. The
driver runs real replicas and clients, keeps every message and reply
ever sent in a list that delivery never removes, so any message can
arrive again, out of order, or never, and takes one caller-chosen step
at a time. After each step it asserts the simulator's safety properties.
The harnesses feed it symbolic choices, so Kani explores every sequence
of them: one request with any delivery, two clients interleaving, and a
one-period primary timeout so view changes fire with a request in
flight. Under a plain cargo build only the driver and its unit tests
compile.

The harness bounds are a first guess and no harness has yet run to
completion; the README says how to run them.

FILE verify/Cargo.toml
@@ -0,0 +1,16 @@

+name = "vsr-verify"
+version = "0.1.0"
+edition = "2021"
+description = "Bounded model checking of vsr-rs with Kani"
+publish = false
+
+[lib]
+name = "vsr_verify"
+path = "lib.rs"
+
+[dependencies]
+vsr-rs = { path = ".." }
+
+[lints.rust]
+unexpected_cfgs = { level = "warn", check-cfg = ['cfg(kani)'] }

FILE verify/cluster.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,372 @@
+//! A small cluster of real replicas and clients, stepped by the caller.
+//!
+//! The cluster is the harness's network. Every message a replica or client
+//! sends goes into a list and stays there: delivering a message does not
+//! remove it, so any message can be delivered again, out of order, or
+//! never. That single rule covers replay, reordering, and loss. A crashed
+//! replica is one that is not stepped for a while, which the caller's
+//! choice of steps already allows.
+//!
+//! The properties asserted by [`Cluster::check`] are the simulator's,
+//! written as plain `assert!` so that Kani checks them.
+
+use std::collections::HashSet;
+use vsr_rs::{Client, ClientID, Config, Message, Replica, ReplicaID, Reply, StateMachine};
+
+/// An operation: a unique id, so a duplicate shows up in a log.
+pub type Op = u32;
+
+/// The replicated state machine. It records every op applied, and answers
+/// with how many ops it has applied so far, so the reply to the op at log
+/// index `i` must carry `i + 1`.
+#[derive(Debug, Default)]
+pub struct Recorder {
+    pub applied: Vec<Op>,
+}
+
+impl StateMachine for Recorder {
+    type Input = Op;
+    type Output = usize;
+
+    fn apply(&mut self, op: Op) -> usize {
+        self.applied.push(op);
+        self.applied.len()
+    }
+}
+
+/// One step of the cluster, chosen by the caller.
+#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
+pub enum Step {
+    /// Deliver the message at this index in [`Cluster::messages`].
+    Deliver(usize),
+    /// Deliver the reply at this index in [`Cluster::replies`] to its client.
+    DeliverReply(usize),
+    /// Run the replica's idle logic: heartbeats, retransmission, timers.
+    Idle(ReplicaID),
+    /// The client sends a new request if it has none pending, otherwise
+    /// re-sends the pending one to every replica.
+    ClientRequest(ClientID),
+}
+
+pub struct Cluster {
+    pub replicas: Vec<Replica<Recorder>>,
+    pub clients: Vec<Client<Op>>,
+    /// Every message sent to a replica so far, with its destination.
+    pub messages: Vec<(ReplicaID, Message<Op>)>,
+    /// Every reply sent to a client so far.
+    pub replies: Vec<Reply<usize>>,
+    /// Whether each client has a request awaiting a reply.
+    pending: Vec<bool>,
+    /// The next op id.
+    next_op: Op,
+    /// Each replica's commit number at the last check.
+    last_commit: Vec<usize>,
+}
+
+impl Cluster {
+    pub fn new(replica_count: usize, client_count: usize, primary_timeout: usize) -> Cluster {
+        let mut config = Config::new();
+        for _ in 0..replica_count {
+            config.add_replica();
+        }
+        config.set_primary_timeout(primary_timeout);
+        let replicas = (0..replica_count)
+            .map(|id| Replica::new(id, config.clone(), Recorder::default()))
+            .collect();
+        let clients = (0..client_count)
+            .map(|id| Client::new(id, config.clone()))
+            .collect();
+        Cluster {
+            replicas,
+            clients,
+            messages: Vec::new(),
+            replies: Vec::new(),
+            pending: vec![false; client_count],
+            next_op: 0,
+            last_commit: vec![0; replica_count],
+        }
+    }
+
+    pub fn replica_count(&self) -> usize {
+        self.replicas.len()
+    }
+
+    pub fn client_count(&self) -> usize {
+        self.clients.len()
+    }
+
+    fn quorum(&self) -> usize {
+        self.replicas.len() / 2 + 1
+    }
+
+    /// Takes one step. Returns `false`, doing nothing, if the step names
+    /// an index that does not exist.
+    pub fn step(&mut self, step: Step) -> bool {
+        match step {
+            Step::Deliver(i) => {
+                let Some((to, message)) = self.messages.get(i).cloned() else {
+                    return false;
+                };
+                self.replicas[to].on_message(message);
+                self.drain_replica(to);
+            }
+            Step::DeliverReply(i) => {
+                let Some(reply) = self.replies.get(i).cloned() else {
+                    return false;
+                };
+                let client = &mut self.clients[reply.client_id];
+                if client.on_reply(reply.request_number, reply.view_number) {
+                    self.pending[reply.client_id] = false;
+                }
+            }
+            Step::Idle(id) => {
+                if id >= self.replicas.len() {
+                    return false;
+                }
+                self.replicas[id].on_idle();
+                self.drain_replica(id);
+            }
+            Step::ClientRequest(id) => {
+                if id >= self.clients.len() {
+                    return false;
+                }
+                if self.pending[id] {
+                    self.clients[id].on_idle();
+                } else {
+                    self.clients[id].on_request(self.next_op);
+                    self.next_op += 1;
+                    self.pending[id] = true;
+                }
+                self.messages.extend(self.clients[id].drain());
+            }
+        }
+        true
+    }
+
+    fn drain_replica(&mut self, id: ReplicaID) {
+        let replica = &mut self.replicas[id];
+        self.messages.extend(replica.drain_messages());
+        self.replies.extend(replica.drain_replies());
+    }
+
+    /// Asserts every property. Call after each step.
+    pub fn check(&mut self) {
+        self.check_commit_numbers();
+        self.check_state_matches_committed_log();
+        self.check_committed_prefix_agreement();
+        self.check_durability();
+        self.check_no_duplicate_ops();
+        self.check_replies_match_commits();
+    }
+
+    /// A replica's commit number never exceeds its log length and never
+    /// decreases.
+    fn check_commit_numbers(&mut self) {
+        for (id, replica) in self.replicas.iter().enumerate() {
+            let commit = replica.commit_number();
+            assert!(
+                commit <= replica.log().len(),
+                "replica {id} commit_number {commit} > log length {}",
+                replica.log().len()
+            );
+            assert!(
+                commit >= self.last_commit[id],
+                "replica {id} commit_number went backwards: {} -> {commit}",
+                self.last_commit[id]
+            );
+            self.last_commit[id] = commit;
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// A replica's state machine has applied exactly the committed prefix
+    /// of its log, in order.
+    fn check_state_matches_committed_log(&self) {
+        for (id, replica) in self.replicas.iter().enumerate() {
+            let commit = replica.commit_number();
+            let applied = &replica.state_machine().applied;
+            assert!(
+                applied.len() == commit,
+                "replica {id} applied {} ops but commit_number is {commit}",
+                applied.len()
+            );
+            for (i, op) in applied.iter().enumerate() {
+                assert!(
+                    *op == replica.log()[i].op,
+                    "replica {id} applied {op} at index {i} but log has {}",
+                    replica.log()[i].op
+                );
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// Two replicas that have both committed index `i` hold the same
+    /// entry there.
+    fn check_committed_prefix_agreement(&self) {
+        for (a, ra) in self.replicas.iter().enumerate() {
+            for (b, rb) in self.replicas.iter().enumerate().skip(a + 1) {
+                let common = ra.commit_number().min(rb.commit_number());
+                for i in 0..common {
+                    assert!(
+                        ra.log()[i] == rb.log()[i],
+                        "replicas {a} and {b} committed different entries at index {i}"
+                    );
+                }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// Every committed entry sits at its index in a quorum of logs.
+    fn check_durability(&self) {
+        let quorum = self.quorum();
+        for (id, replica) in self.replicas.iter().enumerate() {
+            for i in 0..replica.commit_number() {
+                let entry = &replica.log()[i];
+                let copies = self
+                    .replicas
+                    .iter()
+                    .filter(|other| other.log().get(i) == Some(entry))
+                    .count();
+                assert!(
+                    copies >= quorum,
+                    "replica {id} committed index {i} held by {copies} replicas, quorum is {quorum}"
+                );
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// No op appears twice in a committed prefix.
+    fn check_no_duplicate_ops(&self) {
+        for (id, replica) in self.replicas.iter().enumerate() {
+            let mut seen = HashSet::new();
+            for entry in &replica.log()[..replica.commit_number()] {
+                assert!(
+                    seen.insert(entry.op),
+                    "replica {id} committed op {} twice",
+                    entry.op
+                );
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// Every reply answers a committed request with the result of applying
+    /// it. Committed prefixes agree, so the replica that has committed
+    /// furthest has every committed request.
+    fn check_replies_match_commits(&self) {
+        let furthest = self
+            .replicas
+            .iter()
+            .max_by_key(|replica| replica.commit_number())
+            .unwrap();
+        let committed = &furthest.log()[..furthest.commit_number()];
+        for reply in &self.replies {
+            let found = committed.iter().position(|entry| {
+                entry.client_id == reply.client_id && entry.request_number == reply.request_number
+            });
+            let Some(i) = found else {
+                panic!("reply {reply:?} for a request that has not committed");
+            };
+            assert!(
+                reply.result == i + 1,
+                "reply {reply:?} but the request committed at index {i}"
+            );
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+#[cfg(test)]
+mod tests {
+    use super::*;
+
+    /// Delivers messages and replies once each, in order. Keeps its place
+    /// across calls, so nothing is delivered twice.
+    #[derive(Default)]
+    struct InOrder {
+        next_message: usize,
+        next_reply: usize,
+    }
+
+    impl InOrder {
+        /// Delivers everything sent so far, including what gets sent
+        /// along the way, until nothing new is sent.
+        fn deliver_all(&mut self, cluster: &mut Cluster) {
+            while self.next_message < cluster.messages.len()
+                || self.next_reply < cluster.replies.len()
+            {
+                while self.next_message < cluster.messages.len() {
+                    cluster.step(Step::Deliver(self.next_message));
+                    cluster.check();
+                    self.next_message += 1;
+                }
+                while self.next_reply < cluster.replies.len() {
+                    cluster.step(Step::DeliverReply(self.next_reply));
+                    cluster.check();
+                    self.next_reply += 1;
+                }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn requests_commit_everywhere() {
+        let mut cluster = Cluster::new(3, 2, 3);
+        let mut network = InOrder::default();
+        for _ in 0..3 {
+            cluster.step(Step::ClientRequest(0));
+            cluster.step(Step::ClientRequest(1));
+            network.deliver_all(&mut cluster);
+        }
+        // A heartbeat lets the backups commit the last op.
+        cluster.step(Step::Idle(0));
+        network.deliver_all(&mut cluster);
+        for replica in &cluster.replicas {
+            assert_eq!(replica.commit_number(), 6);
+        }
+        assert_eq!(cluster.replies.len(), 6);
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn replayed_request_is_answered_again() {
+        let mut cluster = Cluster::new(3, 1, 3);
+        let mut network = InOrder::default();
+        cluster.step(Step::ClientRequest(0));
+        network.deliver_all(&mut cluster);
+        assert_eq!(cluster.replies.len(), 1);
+        // The request message is at index 0; deliver it again.
+        cluster.step(Step::Deliver(0));
+        cluster.check();
+        assert_eq!(cluster.replies.len(), 2);
+        assert_eq!(cluster.replicas[0].commit_number(), 1);
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn view_change_keeps_committed_ops() {
+        let mut cluster = Cluster::new(3, 1, 1);
+        let mut network = InOrder::default();
+        cluster.step(Step::ClientRequest(0));
+        network.deliver_all(&mut cluster);
+        assert_eq!(cluster.replicas[0].commit_number(), 1);
+        // Replica 0 falls silent. The first idle period clears the
+        // backups' heard-from-primary flag, the second times out.
+        for _ in 0..2 {
+            cluster.step(Step::Idle(1));
+            cluster.step(Step::Idle(2));
+            cluster.check();
+            network.deliver_all(&mut cluster);
+        }
+        for replica in &cluster.replicas {
+            assert_eq!(replica.view_number(), 1);
+            assert_eq!(replica.commit_number(), 1);
+            assert_eq!(replica.log()[0].op, 0);
+        }
+    }
+
+    #[test]
+    fn out_of_range_steps_are_ignored() {
+        let mut cluster = Cluster::new(3, 1, 3);
+        assert!(!cluster.step(Step::Deliver(0)));
+        assert!(!cluster.step(Step::DeliverReply(0)));
+        assert!(!cluster.step(Step::Idle(3)));
+        assert!(!cluster.step(Step::ClientRequest(1)));
+        cluster.check();
+    }
+}

FILE verify/harnesses.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,75 @@
+//! Kani harnesses. Each one runs a cluster for a fixed number of steps,
+//! with every step chosen symbolically, and checks the properties after
+//! each. Kani explores every sequence of choices, so within a harness's
+//! bounds the result is exhaustive.
+//!
+//! The bounds are small on purpose: the state space grows exponentially
+//! in the number of steps, and the hash maps in the replica are expensive
+//! for the solver. `unwind` must cover the longest loop in the harness,
+//! which is the step loop, plus the loops inside the replica and the
+//! standard library that scale with the log and message counts.
+
+use crate::cluster::{Cluster, Step};
+
+/// A symbolic step. Out-of-range indices are assumed away, so a step
+/// kind with nothing to do, such as delivering from an empty message
+/// list, is simply not explored.
+fn any_step(cluster: &Cluster) -> Step {
+    let kind: u8 = kani::any();
+    kani::assume(kind < 4);
+    match kind {
+        0 => {
+            let i: usize = kani::any();
+            kani::assume(i < cluster.messages.len());
+            Step::Deliver(i)
+        }
+        1 => {
+            let i: usize = kani::any();
+            kani::assume(i < cluster.replies.len());
+            Step::DeliverReply(i)
+        }
+        2 => {
+            let id: usize = kani::any();
+            kani::assume(id < cluster.replica_count());
+            Step::Idle(id)
+        }
+        _ => {
+            let id: usize = kani::any();
+            kani::assume(id < cluster.client_count());
+            Step::ClientRequest(id)
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+fn run(replica_count: usize, client_count: usize, primary_timeout: usize, steps: usize) {
+    let mut cluster = Cluster::new(replica_count, client_count, primary_timeout);
+    for _ in 0..steps {
+        let step = any_step(&cluster);
+        assert!(cluster.step(step));
+        cluster.check();
+    }
+}
+
+/// One client, one request, every way its messages can be delivered,
+/// dropped, or replayed, with the primary timeout too long to fire.
+#[kani::proof]
+#[kani::unwind(8)]
+fn one_request_any_delivery() {
+    run(3, 1, 8, 6);
+}
+
+/// Two clients interleaving requests against a stable primary.
+#[kani::proof]
+#[kani::unwind(10)]
+fn two_clients_any_delivery() {
+    run(3, 2, 10, 8);
+}
+
+/// The primary timeout is one idle period, so any idle step on a backup
+/// that has not heard from the primary starts a view change. Every
+/// interleaving of view changes with a request in flight.
+#[kani::proof]
+#[kani::unwind(10)]
+fn view_change_with_request_in_flight() {
+    run(3, 1, 1, 8);
+}

FILE verify/lib.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,22 @@
+//! Bounded model checking of vsr-rs with Kani.
+//!
+//! [`cluster`] drives a small cluster of real replicas and clients. The
+//! caller chooses every step: which message to deliver, which replica to
+//! tick, which client to make a request. After each step the cluster
+//! asserts the same properties the simulator checks. The harnesses in
+//! `harnesses` feed it symbolic choices, so `cargo kani` explores every
+//! delivery order, loss, replay, and view change within the harness bounds.
+//!
+//! Run with `cargo kani -p vsr-verify`, or a single harness with
+//! `cargo kani -p vsr-verify --harness <name>`. Under a plain `cargo build`
+//! only the cluster driver and its unit tests compile.
+
+// Kani's `assert!` takes its message as a plain literal, without
+// interpolation, so the variables named in the messages count as unused
+// under Kani only.
+#![cfg_attr(kani, allow(unused_variables))]
+
+pub mod cluster;
+
+#[cfg(kani)]
+mod harnesses;

COMMIT 467550f155237feda321d7da4093c770b867bdbc
Use B-tree maps and sets instead of hash maps

std's HashMap seeds its hasher at random, so iteration order differs
from run to run. The library only looks entries up, counts them, or
takes a maximum, so its behavior did not depend on the order, but the
Debug output of a replica did, which makes two reproductions of the
same seed harder to diff, and the one tie-break among DoViewChange
votes relied on tied logs being identical. The simulator's reply
property also reported whichever unanswered request came first. With
B-trees everything is ordered by key and a run prints the same thing
every time. The collections are small, so speed is not a concern.

+/// A protocol message between replicas, or from a client to a replica.
+///
+/// Replies from the primary to a client are a [`Reply`], not a message.
+/// Every message from one replica to another carries the sender's view
+/// number, and a replica only acts on normal-case messages whose view
+/// matches its own: a sender that is behind is ignored, one that is ahead
+/// makes the replica catch up first. The variants follow the sections of
+/// the paper: normal operation, state transfer, view changes, recovery.
 #[derive(Clone, Debug)]

+    /// A client asks the primary to execute `op`. Backups ignore it. A
+    /// request number no larger than the client's latest in the primary's
+    /// client table is a re-send: it is answered from the table if it has
+    /// executed, dropped otherwise.
     Request {
         client_id: ClientID,
         request_number: RequestNumber,
         op: Op,
     },
+    /// The primary replicates the request it appended as `op_number` to
+    /// the backups, and tells them how far it has committed so they can
+    /// commit too. Backups accept it only in order: a gap means state
+    /// transfer first. A `Prepare` for an op a backup already has is a
+    /// re-send, and is acknowledged again.
     Prepare {
         view_number: ViewNumber,
         op_number: OpNumber,
         /// The client request being replicated.
         client_id: ClientID,
         request_number: RequestNumber,
         op: Op,
+        /// The primary's commit number.
         commit_number: CommitID,
     },
+    /// A backup tells the primary that it holds every op up to `op_number`.
+    /// With a quorum of these for an op, counting itself and each backup
+    /// once, the primary commits that op and everything before it.

     },
+    /// The primary's heartbeat while there are no requests: it carries the

+    /// notice the primary is gone.

COMMIT 08aeab181ca110aa372df0f8979f6ed9cc99eab2
Let the simulator be observed and driven from outside

For a viewer, the simulator needs to show its state and take faults on
demand. Messages in the network now record who sent them and when they
were sent and are due, Replica exposes its status, and Simulator gains
snapshot(), which describes every replica, client, and message in
flight. Faults can be injected by hand: crash, restart, reboot without
memory, partition a replica off, heal. A fault script, a list of ticks
and faults, drives a run the same way, and parses from text so a
scenario can be saved and replayed. The two-phase run is now steppable
one tick at a time through step_run, with run and run_script on top of
it, so a viewer can advance it at whatever pace it likes.

Injected partitions are cleared when the liveness phase starts, as
every other fault is. Add a test that runs a script with a primary
crash, a reboot, and a partition on an otherwise quiet cluster.

The test found a bug in the liveness core selection from f8acf51: after
taking a quorum of healthy replicas it threw the other healthy ones
away, so a core could never hold more than a quorum of them and
full_core was not full. The remaining slots are now filled from the
other healthy replicas and the recovering ones alike.

@@ -31,7 +31,139 @@ use std::fmt;
 use vsr_rs::{Client, Config, Replica, Reply, RequestNumber};
 
 use network::Network;
-pub use network::{MessageSummary, NetworkOptions};
+pub use network::{message_kind, Envelope, MessageSummary, NetworkOptions, Origin};
+use std::collections::BTreeSet;
+use std::str::FromStr;
+use vsr_rs::{Status, ViewNumber};
+
+/// A fault injected by hand, at a given tick, on top of the random ones.
+#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
+pub enum Fault {
+    /// Crash a replica; it keeps its state, as if paused.
+    Crash(usize),
+    /// Bring a crashed replica back with its state.
+    Restart(usize),
+    /// Bring a crashed replica back with no memory, through recovery.
+    Reboot(usize),
+    /// Cut a replica off from every other replica and client.
+    Partition(usize),
+    /// Reconnect a partitioned replica.
+    Heal(usize),
+    /// Reconnect every partitioned replica.
+    HealAll,
+}
+
+impl fmt::Display for Fault {
+    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
+        match self {
+            Fault::Crash(id) => write!(f, "crash {id}"),
+            Fault::Restart(id) => write!(f, "restart {id}"),
+            Fault::Reboot(id) => write!(f, "reboot {id}"),
+            Fault::Partition(id) => write!(f, "partition {id}"),
+            Fault::Heal(id) => write!(f, "heal {id}"),
+            Fault::HealAll => write!(f, "heal-all"),
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+impl FromStr for Fault {
+    type Err = String;
+
+    fn from_str(s: &str) -> std::result::Result<Fault, String> {
+        let mut words = s.split_whitespace();
+        let name = words.next().ok_or("empty fault")?;
+        let id = |words: &mut std::str::SplitWhitespace| -> std::result::Result<usize, String> {
+            words
+                .next()
+                .ok_or_else(|| format!("{name} needs a replica id"))?
+                .parse()
+                .map_err(|_| format!("{name}: bad replica id"))
+        };
+        match name {
+            "crash" => Ok(Fault::Crash(id(&mut words)?)),
+            "restart" => Ok(Fault::Restart(id(&mut words)?)),
+            "reboot" => Ok(Fault::Reboot(id(&mut words)?)),
+            "partition" => Ok(Fault::Partition(id(&mut words)?)),
+            "heal" => Ok(Fault::Heal(id(&mut words)?)),
+            "heal-all" => Ok(Fault::HealAll),
+            _ => Err(format!("unknown fault {name:?}")),
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+/// A fault script: what to inject, and at which tick.
+pub type FaultScript = Vec<(u64, Fault)>;
+
+/// Parses a script with one `TICK FAULT` per line, `#` starting a comment.
+pub fn parse_script(text: &str) -> std::result::Result<FaultScript, String> {
+    let mut script = Vec::new();
+    for (number, line) in text.lines().enumerate() {
+        let line = line.split('#').next().unwrap().trim();
+        if line.is_empty() {
+            continue;
+        }
+        let (tick, fault) = line
+            .split_once(char::is_whitespace)
+            .ok_or_else(|| format!("line {}: expected TICK FAULT", number + 1))?;
+        let tick = tick
+            .parse()
+            .map_err(|_| format!("line {}: bad tick {tick:?}", number + 1))?;
+        script.push((tick, fault.trim().parse()?));
+    }
+    script.sort_by_key(|(tick, _)| *tick);
+    Ok(script)
+}
+
+/// The phase a run is in. See [`Simulator::step_run`].
+#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
+pub enum Phase {
+    /// Faults are active and the clients are working through their requests.
+    Safety,
+    /// Faults are off and the core has to converge.
+    Liveness,
+    /// The run passed.
+    Done,
+}
+

+    /// Runs one tick of the two-phase run: the safety phase until every
+    /// request is replied to or none has been for `ticks_max_requests`
+    /// ticks, then the liveness phase until the core converges. Returns the
+    /// phase the run is in afterwards, and an error when it has failed.
+    pub fn step_run(&mut self, limits: Limits) -> Result<Phase> {
+        match self.phase {
+            Phase::Safety => {
+                let replied_before = self.requests_replied;
+                self.tick()?;
+                self.ticks_since_reply += 1;
+                if self.requests_replied > replied_before {
+                    self.ticks_since_reply = 0;
+                }
+                let done = self.requests_replied == self.options.requests_max;
+                if done || self.ticks_since_reply >= limits.ticks_max_requests {
+                    self.requests_done = done;
+                    if !done {
+                        info!(
+                            "safety phase ran out of ticks: {} of {} requests replied",
+                            self.requests_replied, self.options.requests_max
+                        );
+                    }
+                    self.transition_to_liveness_mode();
+                    self.phase = Phase::Liveness;
+                }
+            }
+            Phase::Liveness => {
+                if self.liveness_ticks >= limits.ticks_max_convergence {
+                    let reason = self.pending()?.unwrap_or("nothing");
+                    bail!("no state convergence: {reason}");
+                }
+                self.tick()?;
+                self.liveness_ticks += 1;
+                if self.pending()?.is_none() {
+                    if !self.requests_done {
+                        bail!(
+                            "no liveness: only {} of {} requests were replied to",
+                            self.requests_replied,
+                            self.options.requests_max
+                        );
+                    }
+                    self.phase = Phase::Done;
+                }
+            }
+            Phase::Done => {}
         }
+        Ok(self.phase)
+    }

+
+/// Injected faults on a cluster with no random ones: the primary crashes
+/// and comes back, a backup reboots with no memory, a replica is cut off
+/// and reconnected. The run must still pass.
+#[test]
+fn fault_script() {
+    let _ = env_logger::try_init();
+    let mut prng = ChaCha8Rng::seed_from_u64(7);
+    let mut options = Options::lite(&mut prng);
+    options.network = NetworkOptions::perfect();
+    options.replica_crash_probability = 0.0;
+    options.full_core = true;
+    options.requests_max = 20_000;
+    let script = parse_script(
+        "100 crash 0\n\
+         2000 restart 0\n\
+         2500 crash 1\n\
+         2600 reboot 1\n\
+         4000 partition 2\n\
+         # replica 2 is alone for a while\n\
+         5000 heal-all\n",
+    )
+    .unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(script[1], (2000, Fault::Restart(0)));
+    let mut simulator = Simulator::init(7, options).expect("options are valid");
+    if let Err(err) = simulator.run_script(&script, Limits::default()) {
+        panic!("script failed at tick {}: {err:#}", simulator.ticks);
+    }
+    let snapshot = simulator.snapshot();
+    assert!(
+        snapshot.tick > 5000,
+        "the run ended at tick {}",
+        snapshot.tick
+    );
+    assert!(
+        snapshot
+            .replicas
+            .iter()
+            .all(|replica| replica.up && !replica.partitioned),
+        "{:?}",
+        snapshot.replicas
+    );
+    assert!(snapshot
+        .replicas
+        .iter()
+        .any(|replica| replica.view_number > 0));
+    assert_eq!(1, snapshot.reboots);
+}

COMMIT a4d9f790cebc8071af1fb91b496068ae6c2d5f8c
Add a terminal viewer for the simulator

vsr-simulator-tui runs the same simulator and draws it: one row per
replica with its state, status, view, role, op and commit numbers, a
bar of the log with the committed prefix filled, and whether it is in
the liveness core; the messages in flight with their kind, path, and
ticks until delivery; the clients and their pending requests; and an
event log of view changes, status changes, crashes, and injected
faults. Keys pause, single-step, and change speed, select a replica,
and inject faults: crash, restart, reboot without memory, partition
and heal, and cycling packet loss.

--quiet turns the random faults off, --until runs at full speed to a
tick and pauses, and --script replays a fault script. On quit it prints
the seed and every fault injected as a script, so a scenario found by
hand can be replayed headless or pinned as a test. A panic inside the
simulator restores the terminal before printing.

ratatui is the only new dependency, in the simulator crate.

FILE simulator/.tui.rs.swp
[NO API PATCH]

+//! A terminal viewer for the simulator: watch a cluster run, and inject
+//! faults by hand.
+
+use anyhow::Result;
+use clap::Parser;
+use rand::{RngCore, SeedableRng};
+use rand_chacha::ChaCha8Rng;
+use ratatui::crossterm::event::{self, Event, KeyCode, KeyEventKind};
+use ratatui::layout::{Constraint, Layout, Rect};
+use ratatui::style::{Color, Modifier, Style};
+use ratatui::text::{Line, Span};
+use ratatui::widgets::{Block, Cell, Paragraph, Row, Table, Wrap};
+use ratatui::Frame;
+use std::collections::VecDeque;
+use std::time::{Duration, Instant};
+use vsr_rs::Status;
+use vsr_simulator::{
+    message_kind, parse_script, Fault, FaultScript, Limits, NetworkOptions, Options, Origin, Phase,
+    Simulator, Snapshot,
+};
+
+#[derive(Parser)]
+#[command(name = "vsr-simulator-tui")]
+#[command(about = "Watch the simulator run a cluster and inject faults")]
+struct Args {
+    /// Seed as a decimal integer, or a 40-character git commit hash. Read
+    /// from the SEED environment variable if not given; random otherwise.
+    seed: Option<String>,
+    /// Run a small 3-replica cluster with fewer requests.
+    #[arg(long)]
+    lite: bool,
+    /// No random faults: no loss, replay, delay, crashes. Only what you
+    /// inject.
+    #[arg(long)]
+    quiet: bool,
+    /// Start paused.
+    #[arg(long)]
+    paused: bool,
+    /// Ticks per second to start with.
+    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 20.0)]
+    speed: f64,
+    /// Run at full speed to this tick, then pause.
+    #[arg(long)]
+    until: Option<u64>,
+    /// A fault script to replay: one `TICK FAULT` per line.
+    #[arg(long)]
+    script: Option<std::path::PathBuf>,
+    /// Ticks without a reply before the safety phase gives up.
+    #[arg(long, default_value_t = Limits::default().ticks_max_requests)]
+    ticks_max_requests: u64,
+    /// Ticks the liveness phase may take to converge.
+    #[arg(long, default_value_t = Limits::default().ticks_max_convergence)]
+    ticks_max_convergence: u64,
+}
+
+const FRAME: Duration = Duration::from_millis(33);
+const MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME: u64 = 5_000;
+const LOSS_LEVELS: [f64; 4] = [0.0, 0.05, 0.2, 0.5];
+
+struct App {
+    sim: Simulator,
+    seed: u64,
+    limits: Limits,
+    network: NetworkOptions,
+    paused: bool,
+    speed: f64,
+    until: Option<u64>,
+    /// Fraction of a tick carried over between frames.
+    carry: f64,
+    selected: usize,
+    /// Faults still to come from the script given on the command line.
+    scripted: FaultScript,
+    /// Every fault injected, from the script or by hand.
+    injected: FaultScript,
+    events: VecDeque<String>,
+    last: Snapshot,
+    outcome: Option<Result<(), String>>,
+}
+
+impl App {
+    fn log(&mut self, tick: u64, text: impl Into<String>) {
+        self.events.push_back(format!("{tick:>8}  {}", text.into()));
+        while self.events.len() > 500 {
+            self.events.pop_front();
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn inject(&mut self, fault: Fault) {
+        let tick = self.sim.ticks;
+        self.sim.apply(fault);
+        self.injected.push((tick, fault));
+        self.log(tick, format!("inject: {fault}"));
+    }
+
+    /// Advances the run by `ticks`, stopping early at a failure, the end,
+    /// or the `--until` tick.
+    fn advance(&mut self, ticks: u64) {
+        for _ in 0..ticks {
+            if self.outcome.is_some() {
+                return;
+            }
+            while self
+                .scripted
+                .first()
+                .is_some_and(|(at, _)| *at <= self.sim.ticks)
+            {
+                let (_, fault) = self.scripted.remove(0);
+                self.inject(fault);
+            }
+            match self.sim.step_run(self.limits) {
+                Ok(Phase::Done) => {
+                    let tick = self.sim.ticks;
+                    self.log(tick, "PASSED: the core converged");
+                    self.outcome = Some(Ok(()));
+                    self.paused = true;
+                }
+                Ok(_) => {}
+                Err(err) => {
+                    let tick = self.sim.ticks;
+                    self.log(tick, format!("FAILED: {err:#}"));
+                    self.outcome = Some(Err(format!("{err:#}")));
+                    self.paused = true;
+                }
+            }
+            if self.until.is_some_and(|until| self.sim.ticks >= until) {
+                self.until = None;
+                self.paused = true;
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// Notes what changed since the last frame.
+    fn observe(&mut self) {
+        let now = self.sim.snapshot();
+        let tick = now.tick;
+        if now.phase != self.last.phase {
+            let core: Vec<usize> = now
+                .replicas
+                .iter()
+                .filter(|r| r.in_core)
+                .map(|r| r.id)
+                .collect();
+            self.log(tick, format!("{:?} phase, core {core:?}", now.phase));
+        }
+        let mut notes = Vec::new();
+        for (before, after) in self.last.replicas.iter().zip(&now.replicas) {
+            let id = after.id;
+            if after.view_number != before.view_number {
+                notes.push(format!(
+                    "replica {id} moved to view {}{}",
+                    after.view_number,
+                    if after.is_primary { " as primary" } else { "" }
+                ));
+            }
+            if after.status != before.status {
+                notes.push(format!("replica {id} is {}", status_name(after.status)));
+            }
+            if after.up != before.up {
+                notes.push(format!(
+                    "replica {id} {}",
+                    if after.up { "is back up" } else { "crashed" }
+                ));
+            }
+            if after.partitioned != before.partitioned {
+                notes.push(format!(
+                    "replica {id} {}",
+                    if after.partitioned {
+                        "is cut off"
+                    } else {
+                        "is reconnected"
+                    }
+                ));
+            }
+        }
+        for note in notes {
+            self.log(tick, note);
+        }
+        self.last = now;
+    }
+}
+
+fn status_name(status: Status) -> &'static str {
+    match status {
+        Status::Normal => "normal",
+        Status::StateTransfer => "in state transfer",
+        Status::ViewChange => "in view change",
+        Status::Recovering => "recovering",
+    }
+}
+
+fn parse_seed(s: &str) -> Result<u64> {
+    if s.len() == 40 && s.chars().all(|c| c.is_ascii_hexdigit()) {
+        return Ok(u64::from_str_radix(&s[24..], 16)?);
+    }
+    s.parse::<u64>()
+        .map_err(|err| anyhow::anyhow!("invalid seed {s:?}: {err}"))
+}
+
+fn main() -> Result<()> {
+    let args = Args::parse();
+    let seed = match args
+        .seed
+        .clone()
+        .or_else(|| std::env::var("SEED").ok().filter(|s| !s.is_empty()))
+    {
+        Some(s) => parse_seed(&s)?,
+        None => rand::thread_rng().next_u64(),
+    };
+    let mut prng = ChaCha8Rng::seed_from_u64(seed);
+    let mut options = if args.lite {
+        Options::lite(&mut prng)
+    } else {
+        Options::swarm(&mut prng)
+    };
+    if args.quiet {
+        options.network = NetworkOptions::perfect();
+        options.replica_crash_probability = 0.0;
+        options.full_core = true;
+    }
+    let scripted = match &args.script {
+        Some(path) => parse_script(&std::fs::read_to_string(path)?)
+            .map_err(|err| anyhow::anyhow!("{}: {err}", path.display()))?,
+        None => Vec::new(),
+    };
+    let limits = Limits {
+        ticks_max_requests: args.ticks_max_requests,
+        ticks_max_convergence: args.ticks_max_convergence,
+    };
+    let network = options.network.clone();
+    let sim = Simulator::init(seed, options)?;
+    let last = sim.snapshot();
+    let mut app = App {
+        sim,
+        seed,
+        limits,
+        network,
+        paused: args.paused,
+        speed: args.speed.max(0.1),
+        until: args.until,
+        carry: 0.0,
+        selected: 0,
+        scripted,
+        injected: Vec::new(),
+        events: VecDeque::new(),
+        last,
+        outcome: None,
+    };
+    app.log(0, format!("seed {seed}"));
+
+    // A panic inside the simulator must not leave the terminal raw.
+    let hook = std::panic::take_hook();
+    std::panic::set_hook(Box::new(move |info| {
+        ratatui::restore();
+        hook(info);
+    }));
+    let mut terminal = ratatui::init();
+    let result = run(&mut terminal, &mut app);
+    ratatui::restore();
+    result?;
+
+    println!("seed = {}", app.seed);
+    if let Some(outcome) = &app.outcome {
+        match outcome {
+            Ok(()) => println!("passed at tick {}", app.sim.ticks),
+            Err(err) => println!("failed at tick {}: {err}", app.sim.ticks),
+        }
+    } else {
+        println!("stopped at tick {}", app.sim.ticks);
+    }
+    if !app.injected.is_empty() {
+        println!("fault script:");
+        for (tick, fault) in &app.injected {
+            println!("{tick} {fault}");
+        }
+    }
+    Ok(())
+}
+
+fn run(terminal: &mut ratatui::DefaultTerminal, app: &mut App) -> Result<()> {
+    let mut last_frame = Instant::now();
+    loop {
+        app.observe();
+        terminal.draw(|frame| draw(frame, app))?;
+
+        let elapsed = last_frame.elapsed();
+        last_frame = Instant::now();
+        if !app.paused {
+            let ticks = if app.until.is_some() {
+                MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME
+            } else {
+                app.carry += app.speed * elapsed.as_secs_f64();
+                let whole = app.carry.floor();
+                app.carry -= whole;
+                (whole as u64).min(MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME)
+            };
+            app.advance(ticks);
+        }
+
+        if event::poll(FRAME.saturating_sub(last_frame.elapsed()))? {
+            if let Event::Key(key) = event::read()? {
+                if key.kind != KeyEventKind::Press {
+                    continue;
+                }
+                let replicas = app.last.replicas.len();
+                match key.code {
+                    KeyCode::Char('q') | KeyCode::Esc => return Ok(()),
+                    KeyCode::Char(' ') => app.paused = !app.paused,
+                    KeyCode::Char('.') => {
+                        app.paused = true;
+                        app.advance(1);
+                    }
+                    KeyCode::Char('+') | KeyCode::Char('=') => {
+                        app.speed = (app.speed * 2.0).min(100_000.0)
+                    }
+                    KeyCode::Char('-') => app.speed = (app.speed / 2.0).max(0.25),
+                    KeyCode::Char(c @ '1'..='9') => {
+                        let id = c as usize - '1' as usize;
+                        if id < replicas {
+                            app.selected = id;
+                        }
+                    }
+                    KeyCode::Up => app.selected = app.selected.saturating_sub(1),
+                    KeyCode::Down => app.selected = (app.selected + 1).min(replicas - 1),
+                    KeyCode::Char('c') => app.inject(Fault::Crash(app.selected)),
+                    KeyCode::Char('r') => app.inject(Fault::Restart(app.selected)),
+                    KeyCode::Char('R') => app.inject(Fault::Reboot(app.selected)),
+                    KeyCode::Char('p') => {
+                        let fault = if app.sim.is_partitioned(app.selected) {
+                            Fault::Heal(app.selected)
+                        } else {
+                            Fault::Partition(app.selected)
+                        };
+                        app.inject(fault);
+                    }
+                    KeyCode::Char('h') => app.inject(Fault::HealAll),
+                    KeyCode::Char('l') => {
+                        let current = app.network.packet_loss_probability;
+                        let next = LOSS_LEVELS
+                            .iter()
+                            .position(|level| *level > current + 1e-9)
+                            .map(|i| LOSS_LEVELS[i])
+                            .unwrap_or(LOSS_LEVELS[0]);
+                        app.network.packet_loss_probability = next;
+                        app.sim.set_network_options(app.network.clone());
+                        let tick = app.sim.ticks;
+                        app.log(tick, format!("packet loss set to {next}"));
+                    }
+                    _ => {}
+                }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+fn draw(frame: &mut Frame, app: &App) {
+    let snapshot = &app.last;
+    let [header, body, events, keys] = Layout::vertical([
+        Constraint::Length(4),
+        Constraint::Min(8),
+        Constraint::Length(9),
+        Constraint::Length(1),
+    ])
+    .areas(frame.area());
+    draw_header(frame, header, app, snapshot);
+    let [replicas, messages] =
+        Layout::horizontal([Constraint::Percentage(62), Constraint::Percentage(38)]).areas(body);
+    draw_replicas(frame, replicas, app, snapshot);
+    draw_messages(frame, messages, snapshot);
+    draw_events(frame, events, app);
+    let legend = "q quit  space pause  . step  +/- speed  1-9/↑↓ select  c crash  r restart  R reboot  p partition/heal  h heal all  l loss";
+    frame.render_widget(
+        Paragraph::new(legend).style(Style::default().fg(Color::DarkGray)),
+        keys,
+    );
+}
+
+fn draw_header(frame: &mut Frame, area: Rect, app: &App, snapshot: &Snapshot) {
+    let state = match &app.outcome {
+        Some(Ok(())) => Span::styled(
+            "PASSED",
+            Style::default()
+                .fg(Color::Green)
+                .add_modifier(Modifier::BOLD),
+        ),
+        Some(Err(_)) => Span::styled(
+            "FAILED",
+            Style::default().fg(Color::Red).add_modifier(Modifier::BOLD),
+        ),
+        None if app.paused => Span::styled("paused", Style::default().fg(Color::Yellow)),
+        None => Span::styled("running", Style::default().fg(Color::Green)),
+    };
+    let line1 = Line::from(vec![
+        Span::raw(format!("seed {}   tick {}   ", app.seed, snapshot.tick)),
+        Span::raw(format!("{:?} phase   ", snapshot.phase)),
+        state,
+        Span::raw(format!("   {} ticks/s", app.speed)),
+    ]);
+    let net = &snapshot.network;
+    let line2 = Line::from(format!(
+        "requests {}/{} replied of {}   crashes {} restarts {} reboots {}   messages sent {} lost {} replayed {} delayed {}   loss {} replay {} delay {}..{}",
+        snapshot.requests_replied,
+        snapshot.requests_sent,
+        snapshot.requests_max,
+        snapshot.crashes,
+        snapshot.restarts,
+        snapshot.reboots,
+        net.sent,
+        net.lost,
+        net.replayed,
+        net.delayed,
+        app.network.packet_loss_probability,
+        app.network.packet_replay_probability,
+        app.network.one_way_delay_min,
+        app.network.one_way_delay_mean,
+    ));
+    frame.render_widget(
+        Paragraph::new(vec![line1, line2]).block(Block::bordered().title(" vsr-simulator ")),
+        area,
+    );
+}
+
+fn draw_replicas(frame: &mut Frame, area: Rect, app: &App, snapshot: &Snapshot) {
+    let longest = snapshot
+        .replicas
+        .iter()
+        .map(|r| r.op_number)
+        .max()
+        .unwrap_or(0)
+        .max(1);
+    let bar_width = 20usize;
+    let rows = snapshot.replicas.iter().map(|r| {
+        let style = if !r.up {
+            Style::default().fg(Color::DarkGray)
+        } else if r.partitioned {
+            Style::default().fg(Color::Magenta)
+        } else if r.status == Status::Recovering {
+            Style::default().fg(Color::Yellow)
+        } else if r.status != Status::Normal {
+            Style::default().fg(Color::Cyan)
+        } else {
+            Style::default()
+        };
+        let mark = if r.id == app.selected { ">" } else { " " };
+        let state = if !r.up {
+            "down"
+        } else if r.partitioned {
+            "cut off"
+        } else {
+            "up"
+        };
+        let role = if !r.up {
+            ""
+        } else if r.is_primary {
+            "primary"
+        } else {
+            "backup"
+        };
+        let committed = r.commit_number * bar_width / longest;
+        let prepared = r.op_number * bar_width / longest;
+        let bar: String = (0..bar_width)
+            .map(|i| {
+                if i < committed {
+                    '█'
+                } else if i < prepared {
+                    '░'
+                } else {
+                    ' '
+                }
+            })
+            .collect();
+        Row::new(vec![
+            Cell::from(format!("{mark}{}", r.id)),
+            Cell::from(state),
+            Cell::from(status_name(r.status)),
+            Cell::from(format!("{}", r.view_number)),
+            Cell::from(role),
+            Cell::from(format!("{}", r.op_number)),
+            Cell::from(format!("{}", r.commit_number)),
+            Cell::from(bar),
+            Cell::from(format!("{}", r.value)),
+            Cell::from(if r.in_core { "core" } else { "" }),
+        ])
+        .style(style)
+    });
+    let table = Table::new(
+        rows,
+        [
+            Constraint::Length(3),
+            Constraint::Length(8),
+            Constraint::Length(18),
+            Constraint::Length(5),
+            Constraint::Length(8),
+            Constraint::Length(7),
+            Constraint::Length(7),
+            Constraint::Length(bar_width as u16),
+            Constraint::Length(12),
+            Constraint::Length(5),
+        ],
+    )
+    .header(
+        Row::new(vec![
+            "id", "state", "status", "view", "role", "op", "commit", "log", "value", "",
+        ])
+        .style(Style::default().add_modifier(Modifier::BOLD)),
+    )
+    .block(Block::bordered().title(" replicas "));
+    frame.render_widget(table, area);
+
+    // Clients go under the table, in the same box's last lines.
+    let clients: Vec<String> = snapshot
+        .clients
+        .iter()
+        .map(|c| match c.inflight {
+            Some(request) => format!("{}:#{request}", c.id),
+            None => format!("{}:idle", c.id),
+        })
+        .collect();
+    let text = format!(
+        "clients (view they know: {}): {}",
+        snapshot.clients.first().map(|c| c.view_number).unwrap_or(0),
+        clients.join("  ")
+    );
+    let inner = Block::bordered().inner(area);
+    if inner.height > snapshot.replicas.len() as u16 + 2 {
+        let line = Rect {
+            x: inner.x,
+            y: inner.y + inner.height - 1,
+            width: inner.width,
+            height: 1,
+        };
+        frame.render_widget(
+            Paragraph::new(text).style(Style::default().fg(Color::DarkGray)),
+            line,
+        );
+    }
+}
+
+fn draw_messages(frame: &mut Frame, area: Rect, snapshot: &Snapshot) {
+    let now = snapshot.tick;
+    let rows = snapshot.messages.iter().map(|m| {
+        let from = match m.from {
+            Origin::Replica(id) => format!("R{id}"),
+            Origin::Client(id) => format!("C{id}"),
+        };
+        let kind = message_kind(&m.message);
+        let color = match kind {
+            "Prepare" | "PrepareOk" | "Commit" => Color::White,
+            "Request" => Color::Blue,
+            "GetState" | "NewState" => Color::Cyan,
+            "Recovery" | "RecoveryResponse" => Color::Yellow,
+            _ => Color::Magenta,
+        };
+        Row::new(vec![
+            Cell::from(format!("+{}", m.due_at.saturating_sub(now))),
+            Cell::from(kind),
+            Cell::from(format!("{from} → R{}", m.to)),
+        ])
+        .style(Style::default().fg(color))
+    });
+    let table = Table::new(
+        rows,
+        [
+            Constraint::Length(6),
+            Constraint::Length(17),
+            Constraint::Min(9),
+        ],
+    )
+    .header(
+        Row::new(vec!["due", "kind", "path"]).style(Style::default().add_modifier(Modifier::BOLD)),
+    )
+    .block(Block::bordered().title(format!(" in flight: {} ", snapshot.messages.len())));
+    frame.render_widget(table, area);
+}
+
+fn draw_events(frame: &mut Frame, area: Rect, app: &App) {
+    let visible = area.height.saturating_sub(2) as usize;
+    let lines: Vec<Line> = app
+        .events
+        .iter()
+        .rev()
+        .take(visible)
+        .rev()
+        .map(|e| {
+            let style = if e.contains("FAILED") {
+                Style::default().fg(Color::Red)
+            } else if e.contains("PASSED") {
+                Style::default().fg(Color::Green)
+            } else if e.contains("inject") {
+                Style::default().fg(Color::Yellow)
+            } else {
+                Style::default()
+            };
+            Line::styled(e.clone(), style)
+        })
+        .collect();
+    frame.render_widget(
+        Paragraph::new(lines)
+            .wrap(Wrap { trim: false })
+            .block(Block::bordered().title(" events ")),
+        area,
+    );
+}

COMMIT 058368c1ffe60c4449ae2a116b20d8c61a9c01be
Draw the cluster in the viewer, with messages in flight

The viewer now shows the cluster as a picture next to the tables: the
replicas on a ring with replica 0 at the top, the clients in the
middle, faint edges between every pair, and every message in flight as
a letter moving along the edge from its sender to its receiver, placed
by how far it is between its send tick and its due tick and interpolated
within the tick so it glides at low speeds. Letters and colors tell the
kinds apart: P and k for Prepare and PrepareOk, c for Commit, r for a
client request, g and N for state transfer, v, V, and S for the view
change messages, ? and ! for recovery. A node's color and label show
whether it is up, cut off, the primary, recovering, or in a view change
or state transfer, with its view and commit progress underneath.

Quiet mode keeps a small network delay so messages can be seen at all,
and uses a five tick heartbeat, a three period primary timeout, and a
steady request rate, so a crash is acted on within a few seconds at
viewing speed instead of minutes. The side panel drops the state
machine value column so the table fits, and the legend gained a line
explaining the letters.

+/// Where replica `id` of `count` sits on the ring, in canvas coordinates.
+fn ring_position(id: usize, count: usize) -> (f64, f64) {
+    // Replica 0 at the top, the rest clockwise.
+    let angle = std::f64::consts::FRAC_PI_2 - std::f64::consts::TAU * id as f64 / count as f64;
+    (0.72 * angle.cos(), 0.72 * angle.sin())
+}
+
+/// The glyph and color a message in flight is drawn with.
+fn message_glyph(kind: &str) -> (&'static str, Color) {
+    match kind {
+        "Request" => ("r", Color::Blue),
+        "Prepare" => ("P", Color::White),
+        "PrepareOk" => ("k", Color::Green),
+        "Commit" => ("c", Color::DarkGray),
+        "GetState" => ("g", Color::Cyan),
+        "NewState" => ("N", Color::Cyan),
+        "StartViewChange" => ("v", Color::Magenta),
+        "DoViewChange" => ("V", Color::Magenta),
+        "StartView" => ("S", Color::Magenta),
+        "Recovery" => ("?", Color::Yellow),
+        "RecoveryResponse" => ("!", Color::Yellow),
+        _ => ("*", Color::White),
+    }
+}
+
+/// The cluster as a picture: replicas on a ring, clients in the middle,
+/// and every message in flight as a letter moving from sender to
+/// receiver, placed by how far it is between its send and due ticks.
+fn draw_cluster(frame: &mut Frame, area: Rect, app: &App, snapshot: &Snapshot) {
+    let count = snapshot.replicas.len();
+    // Terminal cells are about twice as tall as wide; stretch x so the
+    // ring looks round.
+    let aspect = 2.0 * area.height as f64 / area.width.max(1) as f64;
+    let half_width = 1.0 / aspect.min(1.0);
+    let half_height = aspect.max(1.0);
+    let x_bounds = [-half_width, half_width];
+    let y_bounds = [-half_height, half_height];
+    // Width of one character in canvas units, to center labels.
+    let char_width = (x_bounds[1] - x_bounds[0]) / area.width.max(1) as f64;
+    let line_height = (y_bounds[1] - y_bounds[0]) / area.height.max(1) as f64;
+    let now = snapshot.tick as f64 + app.carry;
+    let canvas = Canvas::default()
+        .block(Block::bordered().title(" cluster "))
+        .x_bounds(x_bounds)
+        .y_bounds(y_bounds)
+        .paint(|ctx: &mut Context| {
+            // Edges between replicas, and to the clients in the middle.
+            for a in 0..count {
+                let (x1, y1) = ring_position(a, count);
+                for b in a + 1..count {
+                    let (x2, y2) = ring_position(b, count);
+                    ctx.draw(&CanvasLine {
+                        x1,
+                        y1,
+                        x2,
+                        y2,
+                        color: Color::Rgb(50, 50, 50),
+                    });
+                }
+            }
+            ctx.layer();
+            // Messages in flight.
+            for m in &snapshot.messages {
+                let (x1, y1) = match m.from {
+                    Origin::Replica(id) => ring_position(id, count),
+                    Origin::Client(_) => (0.0, 0.0),
+                };
+                let (x2, y2) = ring_position(m.to, count);
+                let span = (m.due_at - m.sent_at).max(1) as f64;
+                let t = ((now - m.sent_at as f64) / span).clamp(0.0, 1.0);
+                let (x, y) = (x1 + (x2 - x1) * t, y1 + (y2 - y1) * t);
+                let (glyph, color) = message_glyph(message_kind(&m.message));
+                ctx.print(x, y, Span::styled(glyph, Style::default().fg(color)));
+            }
+            ctx.layer();
+            // Clients in the middle.
+            let waiting = snapshot
+                .clients
+                .iter()
+                .filter(|c| c.inflight.is_some())
+                .count();
+            let label = format!("{} clients, {waiting} waiting", snapshot.clients.len());
+            ctx.print(
+                -(label.len() as f64) * char_width / 2.0,
+                0.0,
+                Span::styled(label, Style::default().fg(Color::Blue)),
+            );
+            // Replicas.
+            for r in &snapshot.replicas {
+                let (x, y) = ring_position(r.id, count);
+                let color = if !r.up {
+                    Color::DarkGray
+                } else if r.partitioned {
+                    Color::Magenta
+                } else if r.status == Status::Recovering {
+                    Color::Yellow
+                } else if r.status != Status::Normal {
+                    Color::Cyan
+                } else if r.is_primary {
+                    Color::Green
+                } else {
+                    Color::White
+                };
+                let mut style = Style::default().fg(color);
+                if r.id == app.selected {
+                    style = style.add_modifier(Modifier::BOLD | Modifier::UNDERLINED);
+                }
+                let name = if !r.up {
+                    format!("[R{} down]", r.id)
+                } else if r.partitioned {
+                    format!("[R{} cut off]", r.id)
+                } else if r.is_primary {
+                    format!("[R{} primary]", r.id)
+                } else {
+                    format!("[R{}]", r.id)
+                };
+                let detail = if r.status == Status::Normal {
+                    format!("v{} {}/{}", r.view_number, r.commit_number, r.op_number)
+                } else {
+                    format!("v{} {}", r.view_number, status_name(r.status))
+                };
+                // The name one row above the node, the detail one row
+                // below, leaving the row the edges meet at clear.
+                ctx.print(
+                    x - name.len() as f64 * char_width / 2.0,
+                    y + line_height,
+                    Span::styled(name, style),
+                );
+                ctx.print(
+                    x - detail.len() as f64 * char_width / 2.0,
+                    y - line_height,
+                    Span::styled(detail, Style::default().fg(color)),
+                );
+            }
+        });
+    frame.render_widget(canvas, area);
+}
+
 fn draw_header(frame: &mut Frame, area: Rect, app: &App, snapshot: &Snapshot) {
     let state = match &app.outcome {
         Some(Ok(())) => Span::styled(

COMMIT ddde7e948319aad2e794dcc94f02cf1b2cf126e4
Viewer: no random faults and one tick per second by default

What you see should be what you did. Random faults are opt-in with
--chaos, which uses the seed's fault configuration like the headless
simulator, and the default speed is one tick per second so messages
can be followed by eye.

COMMIT 502c0653c8d741f149f4aec0b1feabd2abf26273
Viewer: a fixed one-tick transit time by default, not a random delay

The default mode gave messages a random delay of two to six ticks so
they could be seen at all, since a message with no delay is delivered
in the tick it was sent. Random delay reorders messages, which is a
fault, and the header counted it as one. Every message now takes
exactly one tick: visible, in order, deterministic. The network only
counts a message as delayed when it waits longer than the fixed
minimum, and the header calls the minimum what it is, latency.

COMMIT 32181e4da5941ddde7310f4c05c9a7817560ea68
Viewer: replay a seed read-only, or drive an interactive cluster

Two modes instead of flags that blurred them. A seed replays exactly
the run the headless simulator does for it, faults and all, and nothing
can be injected, since that would make it a different run: the fault
keys are inert and the legend says so. --interactive builds a perfect
cluster with no seed, three replicas unless told otherwise, requests
that never run out, one tick of latency so messages can be seen, and
short heartbeat and timeout, where nothing goes wrong until a fault is
injected. On quit it prints the injected faults as a script, which
--script replays on the same cluster.

COMMIT d454ae4a24af9f77bc56fb7fd0bf4c6a5eaead1a
Viewer: messages move again

The tick counter in a snapshot is the number of ticks completed, and a
frame shows the interval before the next one. Measuring a message's
progress from the counter itself put every message at its destination
for the whole tick. Measure from the last completed tick plus the
fraction of the next one that has elapsed, so a message sent in the
last tick glides from sender to receiver over the current one.

     let line_height = (y_bounds[1] - y_bounds[0]) / area.height.max(1) as f64;

+    // frame shows the interval before the next one, `carry` of the way
+    // through it. A message sent in the last completed tick is `carry` of
+    // the way to its receiver.

COMMIT aa3a10bd612f704ee5e6636317ee786fddea2e52
Simulator: make vsr-simulator the default-run binary

Adding the viewer gave the vsr-simulator package a second binary, so
the bare `cargo run -p vsr-simulator` in CI no longer knew which one
to run and failed with "could not determine which binary to run".

COMMIT 19d71e51f41758622b7b00285e6f6634f33e2f8b
Replace the Kani harnesses with a Lean model and a conformance check

The Kani harnesses never finished. The replica keeps five B-trees, and
CBMC unrolls the standard library's node code for every map operation
and even for Drop, so the fully concrete two-step harness ran twenty
minutes and six gigabytes without producing a formula. The symbolic
harnesses branch on top of that, and their unwind bounds sit below the
B-tree node capacity, so they would have failed on unwinding assertions
regardless. Bounded model checking of a whole cluster is the wrong shape
of problem for Kani; the simulator already explores interleavings faster
than CBMC can encode one.

lean/ holds a model of the replica in Lean 4: one definition per Rust
function with the same control flow, B-trees as sorted association lists
so the tie-breaking in the view-change vote matches, and every assert!
as a panicked flag so the handlers are total. The cluster model keeps
every message ever sent, so delivery covers loss, delay, replay, and
reordering with one rule. Safety is stated over reachable states: no
panic, commit bounded by log length, committed-prefix agreement, and
durability in a quorum. The main theorem is a sorry; what is proved is
that commit numbers never move backwards.

verify/ ties the model to the code. It generates seeded traces of cluster
steps, runs the real replicas and the model through each, and diffs what
they print after every step. Forty seeds of two hundred steps pass, on
three and five replicas, reaching view 5, state transfer, and recovery.
Making the model skip commits on Commit messages is caught at step 29 of
seed 0. The test passes with a note when no Lean toolchain is installed.

-//! Kani harnesses. Each one runs a cluster for a fixed number of steps,
-//! with every step chosen symbolically, and checks the properties after
-//! each. Kani explores every sequence of choices, so within a harness's
-//! bounds the result is exhaustive.
-//!
-//! The bounds are small on purpose: the state space grows exponentially
-//! in the number of steps, and the hash maps in the replica are expensive
-//! for the solver. `unwind` must cover the longest loop in the harness,
-//! which is the step loop, plus the loops inside the replica and the
-//! standard library that scale with the log and message counts.
